&\mp\sigma\frac{\partial_{1}(\xi_{d})}{(1+|\partial_{1}\zeta_{0}|^{2})^{3/2}}(\pm\ell)\pm\sigma\mathcal{R}_{z}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta)\partial_{1}\xi_{d}({\pm\ell})=\kappa v_{d}\cdot\mathcal{N}(\pm\ell)-F^{7}(\pm\ell),\end{aligned}\right.

is given by

(3.14)

\displaystyle\int_{-\ell}^{\ell}{g}(\xi_{d})\vartheta+\sigma\frac{\partial_{1}(\xi_{d})\partial_{1}\vartheta}{(1+|\partial_{1}\zeta_{0}|^{2})^{3/2}}+\sigma\int_{-\ell}^{\ell}(\mathcal{R}_{z}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta)\partial_{1}\xi_{d}ds)\partial_{1}\vartheta

\displaystyle=\int_{-\ell}^{\ell}S_{\mathcal{A}}(\overset{\circ}{q}_{d},v_{d})\mathcal{N}\cdot\frac{\mathcal{N}}{|\mathcal{N}|^{2}}\vartheta+\int_{-\ell}^{\ell}\nabla_{\mathcal{A}}(Rv)\mathcal{N}\cdot\frac{\mathcal{N}}{|\mathcal{N}|^{2}}-[v_{d}\cdot\mathcal{N},\vartheta]_{\ell}+[F^{7},\vartheta]_{\ell}

for each
\vartheta\in H^{1}((-\ell,\ell))
. Just like Step 2, system (
3.13
) can be viewed as a linear perturbation of the elliptic system:

(3.15)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &g(\xi_{d})-\sigma\partial_{1}\left(\frac{\partial_{1}(\xi_{d})}{(1+|\partial_{1}\zeta_{0}|^{2})^{3/2}}\right)=\left(S_{\mathcal{A}}(\overset{\circ}{q}_{d},v_{d})\mathcal{N}\right)\cdot\frac{\mathcal{N}}{|\mathcal{N}|^{2}}+F^{4}\cdot\mathcal{N}+\nabla_{\mathcal{A}}(Rv_{d})\mathcal{N}\cdot\frac{\mathcal{N}}{|\mathcal{N}|^{2}}\quad\text{in}\ (-\ell,\ell),\\
&\mp\sigma\frac{\partial_{1}(\xi_{d})}{(1+|\partial_{1}\zeta_{0}|^{2})^{3/2}}(\pm\ell)=\kappa v_{d}\cdot\mathcal{N}(\pm\ell)-F^{7}(\pm\ell),\end{aligned}\right.

By the elliptic estimates of (
3.15
),
we have

(3.16)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \|\xi_{d}(t)\|_{L^{2}W^{3-1/q_{-},q_{-}}}^{2}&\lesssim\|\overset{\circ}{q}_{d}\|_{L^{2}W^{1-1/q_{-},q_{-}}(-\ell,\ell)}^{2}+\|v_{d}\|_{L^{2}W^{2-1/q_{-},q_{-}}(-\ell,\ell)}^{2}+||[F^{7}]_{\ell}||_{L_{t}^{2}}^{2}.\end{aligned}

For the perturbation terms, by Hölder’s inequality, we have

\|\partial_{1}(\mathcal{R}_{z}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta))\partial_{1}\xi_{d}\|_{L_{t}^{2}W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{-}},q_{-}}}\lesssim\|\eta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{-}},q_{-}}}\|\xi_{d}\|_{L_{t}^{2}W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{-}},q_{-}}},

and similarly

[\mathcal{R}_{z}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta)\partial_{1}\xi_{d}]_{\ell}\lesssim\|\eta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{-}},q_{-}}}\|\xi_{d}\|_{L_{t}^{2}W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{-}},q_{-}}}.

Therefore, using the similar argument as in Step 2 by regarding (
3.14
) as a perturbation of (
3.15
), and the smallness of
||\eta||_{L^{2}W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{-}},q_{-}}}
, we deduce that
\xi
is a strong solution to (
3.13
) which obeys (
3.16
).

Step 4 –The Estimate of
\bar{q}_{d}

From the previous two steps, in order to complete the elliptic estimate, it remains to derive the boundedness for
\bar{q}_{d}
which is the mean value of
q_{d}
. We aim to derive the estimate for
\bar{q}_{d}
from the capillary equation

S_{\mathcal{A}}(q_{d},v_{d})\mathcal{N}=\mathcal{K}(\xi)\mathcal{N}+(\partial_{1}(\mathcal{R}_{z}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta)\partial_{1}\xi_{d}))\mathcal{N}+F^{4}+\nabla_{\mathcal{A}}(Rv)\mathcal{N}.

Testing this equation with the function
\psi=M\nabla\tilde{\phi}
with
\tilde{\phi}
solving the following system

(3.17)

\displaystyle{}\Delta\tilde{\phi}=g\frac{2\ell}{|\Omega|}\bar{q}_{d}~\operatorname{in}~\Omega,\quad\tilde{\phi}\cdot\nu=\frac{g\bar{q}_{d}}{|\mathcal{N}_{0}|}~\operatorname{on}~\Sigma,\quad\tilde{\phi}\cdot\nu=0~\operatorname{on}~\Sigma_{s},

and using integration by parts, we obtain

(3.18)

\displaystyle{}2g\ell|\bar{q}_{d}|^{2}=(\xi_{d},\psi\cdot\mathcal{N})_{1,\Sigma}-(S_{\mathcal{A}}(q_{d}-\bar{q}_{d},v_{d}+Rv)\mathcal{N},\psi)_{L^{2}}+\kappa[v\cdot\mathcal{N},\psi\cdot\mathcal{N}]_{\ell}-[F^{7},\psi\cdot\mathcal{N}]_{\ell}.

For the term
(S_{\mathcal{A}}(q_{d}-\bar{q}_{d},v_{d}+Rv)\mathcal{N},\psi)_{L^{2}}
, we obtain the following from equation
\partial_{t}v_{d}+\operatorname{div}_{\mathcal{A}}(S_{\mathcal{A}}(q_{d},v_{d}+Rv))=F^{1}
and the fact that
v_{d}+Rv=\partial_{t}v

(3.19)

\displaystyle{}\begin{aligned} (S_{\mathcal{A}}(q_{d}-\bar{q}_{d},\partial_{t}v)\mathcal{N},\psi)_{L^{2}}=-\int_{\Omega}(\partial_{t}^{2}v)\psi J+\int_{\Omega}JF^{1}\psi+\int_{\Omega}S_{\mathcal{A}}(q_{d}-\bar{q}_{d},\partial_{t}v):D_{\mathcal{A}^{n}}\psi J\\
-\int_{\Sigma_{s}}(S_{\mathcal{A}^{n}}(q_{d}-\bar{q}_{d},\partial_{t}v)\nu\cdot\tau)\psi\cdot\tau J.\end{aligned}

The elliptic estimate for (
3.17
) yields that

\|\psi\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}\lesssim|\bar{q}_{d}|.

Therefore, applying this result and (
3.19
) to equation (
3.18
), and using trace theorem, we obtain

\displaystyle 2g\ell|\bar{q}_{d}|^{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle g^{2}|\bar{q}_{d}|\int_{-\ell}^{\ell}|\xi_{d}|dx+|\bar{q}_{d}|(\|v_{d}\|_{H^{1}}+\|\partial_{t}v_{d}\|_{L^{2}}+\|q_{d}-\bar{q}_{d}\|_{L^{2}}+\|F^{1}\|_{L^{q_{-}}})

\displaystyle+|[v_{d}\cdot\mathcal{N}]_{\ell}||\bar{q}_{d}|+|[F^{7}]_{\ell}||\bar{q}_{d}|,

which implies that

(3.20)

\displaystyle{}2g\ell|\bar{q}_{d}|\lesssim g^{2}\int_{-\ell}^{\ell}|\xi_{d}|dx+(\|v_{d}\|_{H^{1}}+\|\partial_{t}v_{d}\|_{L^{2}}+\|q_{d}-\bar{q}_{d}\|_{W^{1,q_{-}}}+\|F^{1}\|_{L^{q_{-}}})

\displaystyle+[v_{d}\cdot\mathcal{N}]_{\ell}+[F^{7}]_{\ell}.

Therefore, we have

|\bar{q}_{d}|_{L_{t}^{2}}\lesssim\|\xi_{d}\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}+(\|v_{d}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H^{1}}+\|\partial_{t}v_{d}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}+\|q_{d}-\bar{q}_{d}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}+\|F^{1}\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{q_{-}}})+\|[v_{d}\cdot\mathcal{N}]_{\ell}\|_{L_{t}^{2}}+\||[F^{7}]_{\ell}|\|_{L_{t}^{2}},

and

|\bar{q}_{d}|_{L_{t}^{\infty}}\lesssim\|\xi_{d}\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}+(\|v_{d}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H^{1}}+\|\partial_{t}v_{d}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L^{2}}+\|q_{d}-\bar{q}_{d}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L^{2}}+\|F^{1}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}^{1})^{*}})+\|[v_{d}\cdot\mathcal{N}]_{\ell}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}}+\||[F^{7}]_{\ell}|\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}}.

Combining Step 2 – Step 4, we obtain the elliptic estimate (
3.5
), and the estimates (
3.3
) and (
3.4
) for
q_{d}
.

Step 5 – Strong Solution to (
1.28
)

We now use the weak formulation with pressure to derive the strong formulation. Since we already have the estimate for
v_{d}
, we then solve the ODE (
1.31
) to recover
(v,q,\xi)
and use
(D_{t}v,\partial_{t}\xi,\partial_{t}p)
to substitute
(v_{d},\xi_{d},q_{d})
. We first integrate
((\partial_{t}v,\psi))-(\partial_{t}q,\mathop{\rm div}\nolimits_{\mathcal{A}}\psi)_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}
by parts to deduce that

(3.21)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &(\partial_{t}^{2}v,\psi)_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}-\mu(\Delta_{\mathcal{A}}\partial_{t}v,\psi)_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}+(\nabla_{\mathcal{A}}\partial_{t}q,\psi)_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}+(\partial_{t}\xi,\psi\cdot\mathcal{N})_{1,\Sigma_{0}}+(\mathcal{R}_{z}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta)\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi,\partial_{1}(\psi\cdot\mathcal{N}))_{L^{2}}\\
&+[\partial_{t}v\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n},\psi\cdot\mathcal{N}]_{\ell}=\int_{\Omega}F^{1}\cdot\psi J^{n}+\int_{-\ell}^{\ell}S_{\mathcal{A}}(\partial_{t}q,\partial_{t}v)\mathcal{N}\cdot\psi\\
&\quad+\int_{\Sigma_{s}}\left(S_{\mathcal{A}}(\partial_{t}q,\partial_{t}v)\nu\cdot\tau\right)(\psi\cdot\tau)J-\beta\int_{\Sigma_{s}}(\partial_{t}v\cdot\tau)(\psi\cdot\tau)J^{n}-[\psi\cdot\mathcal{N},F^{7}]_{\ell}.\end{aligned}

If we restrict
\psi\in C_{c}^{1}(\Omega)\subset\mathcal{W}(t)
, all boundary terms in (
3.21
) vanish, so that
\partial_{t}^{2}v+\nabla_{\mathcal{A}}\partial_{t}q-\Delta_{\mathcal{A}}\partial_{t}v=F^{1}
in
\Omega
. By plugging this into (
3.21
) and integrating by parts over
(-\ell,\ell)
, we have

(3.22)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\bigg(g(\partial_{t}\xi)-\sigma\partial_{1}\Big(\frac{\partial_{1}(\partial_{t}\xi)}{(1+(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0})^{2})^{3/2}}\Big)-\sigma\partial_{1}(\mathcal{R}_{z}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta)\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi),\psi\cdot\mathcal{N}\bigg)_{L^{2}}+\sigma\frac{\partial_{1}(\partial_{t}\xi)}{(1+(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0})^{2})^{3/2}}(\psi\cdot\mathcal{N})\Big|_{-\ell}^{\ell}\\
&+[\partial_{t}v\cdot\mathcal{N},\psi\cdot\mathcal{N}]_{\ell}-[\mathcal{R}_{z}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta)\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi,\psi\cdot\mathcal{N}]_{\ell}=\int_{-\ell}^{\ell}S_{\mathcal{A}}(\partial_{t}q,\partial_{t}v)\mathcal{N}\cdot\psi-F^{4}\cdot\psi\\
&\quad+\int_{\Sigma_{s}}\left(S_{\mathcal{A}}(\partial_{t}q,\partial_{t}v)\nu\cdot\tau\right)(\psi\cdot\tau)J-\int_{\Sigma_{s}}F^{5}(\psi\cdot\tau)J-\beta\int_{\Sigma_{s}}(\partial_{t}v\cdot\tau)(\psi\cdot\tau)J-[\psi\cdot\mathcal{N},F^{7}]_{\ell}.\end{aligned}

Restricting
\psi\in\mathcal{W}_{\sigma}(t)\cap\{\psi|\psi\cdot\mathcal{N}\in C^{1}_{c}(-\ell,\ell),\psi=0\ \text{on}\ \Sigma_{s}\}
, we deduce stress tensor boundary condition
S_{\mathcal{A}}(\partial_{t}q,\partial_{t}v)\mathcal{N}=\left(g(\partial_{t}\xi)-\sigma\partial_{1}\Big(\frac{\partial_{1}(\partial_{t}\xi)}{(1+(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0})^{2})^{3/2}}\Big)-\sigma\partial_{1}(\mathcal{R}_{z}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta)\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi)\right)\mathcal{N}
. Here
\psi
obeys the zero average constraint
\int_{-\ell}^{\ell}(\psi\cdot\mathcal{N})=0
, which results in a constant coming from the term
(\partial_{t}\xi,\psi\cdot\mathcal{N})_{1,\Sigma_{0}}
after integration by parts on
(-\ell,\ell)
. Similarly, we can restrict
\psi\in\mathcal{W}(t)\cap\{\psi|\psi|_{\Sigma_{s}}\in C^{1}_{c}(\Sigma_{s})\}
to deduce the boundary condition
S_{\mathcal{A}}(\partial_{t}q,\partial_{t}v)\nu\cdot\tau-\beta(\partial_{t}v\cdot\tau)=F^{5}
on
\Sigma_{s}
.

Finally, the remaining boundary conditions at contact points in (
3.22
) are

(3.23)

\displaystyle\sigma\frac{\partial_{1}(\partial_{t}\xi)}{(1+(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0})^{2})^{3/2}}(\psi\cdot\mathcal{N})\Big|_{-\ell}^{\ell}+[\partial_{t}v\cdot\mathcal{N},\psi\cdot\mathcal{N}]_{\ell}-[\mathcal{R}_{z}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta)\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi,\psi\cdot\mathcal{N}]_{\ell}=-[\psi\cdot\mathcal{N},F^{7}]_{\ell}.

If we restrict
\psi\in\mathcal{W}_{\sigma}(t)
and
\psi\cdot\mathcal{N}
supported on
-\ell
, (
3.23
) gives the contact point condition on
-\ell
. Moreover, (
3.23
) with restriction of
\psi\in\mathcal{W}(t)
and
\psi\cdot\mathcal{N}
supported on
\ell
gives the contact point condition on
\ell
.

Using the definition of
v
, we derive the strong form of system (
1.28
) for
(v_{d},q_{d},\xi_{d})
. This finishes the proof.
∎

From Theorem
3.5
, we have the following corollary.

Theorem 3.6
.

Suppose that we have the weak solution
(v_{d},\xi_{d})\in L^{\infty}H^{0}\times\left(L^{2}H^{1}\cap L^{2}H^{3/2-\alpha}\right)
to the smooth given data system (
1.29
) on the finite time interval
[0,T]
,
j=0,1
. Then there exists a unique pressure
q_{d}\in L^{\infty}([0,T];H^{0}(\Omega))\cap L^{2}([0,T];W^{1,q_{-}})
satisfying the estimate (
3.3
) and (
3.4
). Moreover
(v_{d},q_{d},\xi_{d})
is the strong solution to (
1.28
), and obeys the following estimate

(3.24)

\displaystyle\|v_{d}\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{-}}}^{2}+\|q_{d}\|_{L^{2}W^{1,q_{-}}}^{2}+\|\xi_{d}\|_{L^{2}W^{3-1/q_{-},q_{-}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\partial_{t}v_{d}\|_{L^{2}H^{0}}^{2}+\|\xi_{d}\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}^{2}+\|v_{d}\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}^{2}+\|\xi_{d}\|_{L^{2}H^{3/2-\alpha}}^{2}+\|[v_{d}\cdot\mathcal{N}]_{\ell}\|_{L^{2}_{t}}^{2}+\|F^{1}\|_{L^{2}L^{q_{-}}}^{2}+\|F^{4}\|_{L^{2}W^{1-1/q_{-},q_{-}}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|F^{5}\|_{L^{2}W^{1-1/q_{-},q_{-}}}^{2}+\|[F^{7}]_{\ell}\|_{L_{t}^{2}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\eta^{n}\|^{2}_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{-}},q_{-}}}\|v_{l}^{k}\|_{L_{t}^{2}W^{2,q_{-}}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}u^{k}\|_{L_{t}^{2}W^{2,q_{-}}}^{2}\|\partial_{t}\xi_{l}^{k}\|_{L_{t}^{2}W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{-}},q_{-}}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{t}\eta^{n}\|_{L_{t}^{2}W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{-}},q_{-}}}^{2}\|\partial_{t}\xi_{l}^{k}\|_{L_{t}^{2}W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{-}},q_{-}}}^{2}.

Proof.

The proof is similar to that of Theorem
3.5
. We omit the details here.
∎

3.3.
Elliptic Estimates

In order to pass to the limit
n\to+\infty
and recover the original system (
1.17
), we need to establish
q_{+}
-elliptic estimates for the zero-order terms
(v,q,\xi)
. This is achieved in the following theorem.

Theorem 3.7
.

Suppose that
(\partial_{t}v,\partial_{t}q,\partial_{t}\xi)\in L_{t}^{2}W^{2,q_{-}}(\Omega)\times L_{t}^{2}W^{1,q_{-}}(\Omega)\times L_{t}^{2}W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{-}},q_{-}}
, and that
(v,q,\xi)
is the strong solution to the following equation

(3.25)

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}^{2}v+\operatorname{div}_{\mathcal{A}^{n}}S_{\mathcal{A}^{n}}(\partial_{t}v,\partial_{t}q)=F^{1}(u,p,\eta)~~~&\operatorname{in}~~\Omega(t),\\
\operatorname{div}_{\mathcal{A}^{n}}D_{t}v=0~~~&\operatorname{in}~~\Omega(t),\\
S_{\mathcal{A}^{n}}(\partial_{t}q,\partial_{t}v)\mathcal{N}^{n}=g\partial_{t}\xi\mathcal{N}^{n}-\sigma\partial_{1}\left(\frac{\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi}{(1+|\partial_{1}\zeta_{0}|^{2})^{3/2}}\right)\mathcal{N}^{n}\\
\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+\sigma\partial_{1}(\mathcal{R}_{z}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta)\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi)\mathcal{N}^{n}+F^{4}(u,p,\eta)~~~&\operatorname{on}~~\Sigma(t),\\
(S_{\mathcal{A}^{n}}(\partial_{t}q,\partial_{t}v)\nu-\beta\partial_{t}v)\cdot\tau=F^{5}(u,\eta,p)~~~&\operatorname{on}~~\Sigma_{s}(t),\\
\partial_{t}v\cdot\nu=0~~~&\operatorname{on}~~\Sigma_{s}(t),\\
\partial_{t}^{2}\xi=\partial_{t}v\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}+u_{1}\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi~~~&\operatorname{on}~~\Sigma(t),\\
\sigma(\mp\frac{\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi}{(1+|\partial_{1}\zeta_{0}|^{2})^{\frac{3}{2}}}\pm\mathcal{R}_{z}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta)\partial_{t}\partial_{1}\xi)(\pm\ell)=\kappa(\partial_{t}v\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n})(\pm\ell)-{F}^{7}.\end{cases}

Given that
\int_{0}^{t}F^{1}(u,p,\eta)\in L_{t}^{\infty}L^{q_{+}}(\Omega)
,
\int_{0}^{t}F^{4}(u,p,\eta)\in L_{t}^{\infty}W^{1-\frac{1}{q_{+}},q_{+}}(\Sigma)
,
\int_{0}^{t}F^{5}(u,p,\eta)\in L_{t}^{\infty}W^{1-\frac{1}{q_{+}},q_{+}}(\Sigma)
,
\int_{0}^{t}[F^{7}(u,\eta,p)]_{\ell}\in L_{t}^{\infty}
,
and
F^{1,0}(u_{0},p_{0},\eta_{0})\in L^{q_{+}}(\Omega)
,
where the definitions of
F^{i}
are given in Appendix
A
, and the definition of
F^{1,0}
is given by (
B.5
) in Appendix
B
, it follows that
(\partial_{t}v,\partial_{t}q,\partial_{t}\xi)
satisfies the following estimate.

(3.26)

\displaystyle\|v\|_{L^{\infty}W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|q\|_{L^{\infty}W^{1,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\xi\|_{L^{\infty}W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\partial_{t}v\|_{L^{\infty}H^{0}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\xi\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}^{2}+\|v\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}^{2}+\|\xi\|_{L^{\infty}H^{3/2-\alpha}}^{2}+\|[v\cdot\mathcal{N}]_{\ell}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}}^{2}+\left\|\int_{0}^{t}F^{1}\right\|_{L^{\infty}L^{q_{+}}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\left\|\int_{0}^{t}F^{4}\right\|_{L^{\infty}W^{1-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}+\left\|\int_{0}^{t}F^{5}\right\|_{L^{\infty}W^{1-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}+\left\|\int_{0}^{t}[F^{7}]_{\ell}\right\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}}^{2}+\|F^{1,0}\|_{L^{q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\eta_{0}\|_{W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{+}},q_{+}}}^{4}.

Proof.

Integrating the equation (
3.25
) from
0
to
t
, and using the compatibility condition for the initial condition, we obtain the following equation

(3.27)

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}v+\operatorname{div}_{\mathcal{A}^{n}}S_{\mathcal{A}^{n}}(v,q)=\int_{0}^{t}F^{1}(u,p,\eta)+\int_{0}^{t}\operatorname{div}_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}^{n}}S_{\mathcal{A}^{n}}(v,q)+\int_{0}^{t}\operatorname{div}_{\mathcal{A}^{n}}S_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}^{n}}(v,q)\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+F^{1,0}(u(0),p(0),\eta(0))~~~&\operatorname{in}~~\Omega(t),\\
\operatorname{div}_{\mathcal{A}^{n}}v=0~~~&\operatorname{in}~~\Omega(t),\\
S_{\mathcal{A}^{n}}(q,v)\mathcal{N}^{n}=g\xi\mathcal{N}^{n}-\sigma\partial_{1}\left(\frac{\partial_{1}\xi}{(1+|\partial_{1}\zeta_{0}|^{2})^{3/2}}\right)\mathcal{N}^{n}+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{K}(\xi)\partial_{t}\mathcal{N}^{n}+\int_{0}^{t}S_{\mathcal{A}^{n}}(v,q)\partial_{t}\mathcal{N}^{n}\\
\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+\partial_{1}(\mathcal{R}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta_{0}))+\sigma\int_{0}^{t}\partial_{1}(\mathcal{R}_{z}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta)\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi)\mathcal{N}^{n}+\int_{0}^{t}F^{4}(u,p,\eta)\\
\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+\int_{0}^{t}S_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}^{n}}(q,v)\mathcal{N}^{n}~~~&\operatorname{on}~~\Sigma(t),\\
(S_{\mathcal{A}^{n}}(q,v)\nu-\beta v)\cdot\tau=\int_{0}^{t}F^{5}(u,\eta,p)+\int_{0}^{t}(S_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}^{n}}(q,v)\nu)\cdot\tau~~~&\operatorname{on}~~\Sigma_{s}(t),\\
v\cdot\nu=0~~~&\operatorname{on}~~\Sigma_{s}(t),\\
\partial_{t}\xi=v\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}-\int_{0}^{t}v\cdot\partial_{t}\mathcal{N}^{n}+\int_{0}^{t}(u_{1}\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi)~~~&\operatorname{on}~~\Sigma(t),\\
\sigma(\mp\frac{\partial_{1}\xi}{(1+|\partial_{1}\zeta_{0}|^{2})^{\frac{3}{2}}}\mp\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{R}_{z}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta)\partial_{t}\partial_{1}\xi(\pm\ell))=\kappa(v\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n})(\pm\ell)-\int_{0}^{t}{F}^{7}.\end{cases}

Following an argument similar to that in the proof of Theorem
3.5
, we regard system (
3.27
) as a perturbation of the following system

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}v+\operatorname{div}_{\mathcal{A}^{n}}S_{\mathcal{A}^{n}}(v,q)=\int_{0}^{t}F^{1}(u,p,\eta)+F^{1,0}(u_{0},\eta_{0},p_{0})~~~&\operatorname{in}~~\Omega(t),\\
\operatorname{div}_{\mathcal{A}^{n}}v=0~~~&\operatorname{in}~~\Omega(t),\\
S_{\mathcal{A}^{n}}(q,v)\mathcal{N}^{n}=g\xi\mathcal{N}^{n}-\sigma\partial_{1}\left(\frac{\partial_{1}\xi}{(1+|\partial_{1}\zeta_{0}|^{2})^{3/2}}\right)\mathcal{N}^{n}+\int_{0}^{t}F^{4}(u,p,\eta)+\partial_{1}(\mathcal{R}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta_{0}))~~~&\operatorname{on}~~\Sigma(t),\\
(S_{\mathcal{A}^{n}}(q,v)\nu-\beta v)\cdot\tau=\int_{0}^{t}F^{5}(u,\eta,p)~~~&\operatorname{on}~~\Sigma_{s}(t),\\
v\cdot\nu=0~~~&\operatorname{on}~~\Sigma_{s}(t),\\
\partial_{t}\xi=v\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}~~~&\operatorname{on}~~\Sigma(t),\\
\sigma(\mp\frac{\partial_{1}\xi}{(1+|\partial_{1}\zeta_{0}|^{2})^{\frac{3}{2}}})(\pm\ell)=\kappa(v\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n})(\pm\ell)-\int_{0}^{t}{F}^{7}.\end{cases}

Therefore, it remains to estimate the additional terms and show that they act as small perturbations. We begin with the Navier–Stokes equation, for which the following estimate follows from Hölder’s inequality:

\displaystyle\left\|\int_{0}^{t}\operatorname{div}_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}^{n}}S_{\mathcal{A}^{n}}(v,q)\right\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L^{q_{+}}}\lesssim

\displaystyle T\|\partial_{t}\eta^{n}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{-}},q_{-}}}(1+\|\eta^{n}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{+}},q_{+}}})(\|v\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{2,q_{+}}}+\|q\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{1,q_{+}}}),

\displaystyle\left\|\int_{0}^{t}\operatorname{div}_{\mathcal{A}^{n}}S_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}^{n}}(v,q)\right\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L^{q_{+}}}\lesssim

\displaystyle T\|\partial_{t}\eta^{n}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{-}},q_{-}}}(1+\|\eta^{n}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{+}},q_{+}}})(\|v\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{2,q_{+}}}+\|q\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{1,q_{+}}}).

For the capillary boundary condition, by temporal integration by part, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{K}(\xi)\partial_{t}\mathcal{N}^{n}\right\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{1-\frac{1}{q_{+}},q_{+}}}\lesssim\|\xi\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{+}},q_{+}}}\|\partial_{t}\eta^{n}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{-}},q_{-}}}\lesssim\|\xi\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{+}},q_{+}}}\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{-}},q_{-}}},

\displaystyle\left\|\int_{0}^{t}S_{\mathcal{A}^{n}}(v,q)\partial_{t}\mathcal{N}^{n}\right\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{1-\frac{1}{q_{+}},q_{+}}}\lesssim(\|v\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{2,q_{+}}}+\|q\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{1,q_{+}}})\|\partial_{t}\eta^{n}\|_{L_{t}^{2}W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{-}},q_{-}}},

\displaystyle\left\|\int_{0}^{t}S_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}^{n}}(v,q)\mathcal{N}^{n}\right\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{1-\frac{1}{q_{+}},q_{+}}}\lesssim(\|v\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{2,q_{+}}}+\|q\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{1,q_{+}}})\|\partial_{t}\eta^{n}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{-}},q_{-}}},

and

\displaystyle\left\|\int_{0}^{t}\sigma\partial_{1}(\mathcal{R}_{z}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta)\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi)\mathcal{N}^{n}\right\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{1-\frac{1}{q_{+}},q_{+}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\int_{0}^{t}(\mathcal{R}_{z}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta)\partial_{1}^{2}\partial_{t}\xi)\right\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{1-\frac{1}{q_{+}},q_{+}}}+\left\|\int_{0}^{t}(\mathcal{R}_{zz}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta)\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi\partial_{1}^{2}\eta)\right\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{1-\frac{1}{q_{+}},q_{+}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\sigma\int_{0}^{t}(\mathcal{R}_{zz}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta)\partial_{1}^{2}\xi\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\eta)\mathcal{N}^{n}\right\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{1-\frac{1}{q_{+}},q_{+}}}+\|\sigma\partial_{1}(\mathcal{R}_{z}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta)\partial_{1}\xi)\mathcal{N}^{n}(0)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{1-\frac{1}{q_{+}},q_{+}}}

\displaystyle+\|\sigma\partial_{1}(\mathcal{R}_{z}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta)\partial_{1}\xi)\mathcal{N}^{n}(T)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{1-\frac{1}{q_{+}},q_{+}}}+\left\|\int_{0}^{t}(\mathcal{R}_{zz}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta)\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi\partial_{1}^{2}\eta)\right\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{1-\frac{1}{q_{+}},q_{+}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim T\|\partial_{t}\partial_{1}\xi\|_{L_{t}^{2}W^{\max(\frac{1}{q_{+}},1-\frac{1}{q_{+}}),q_{+}}}\|\partial_{1}\eta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{2-\frac{1}{q_{+}},q_{+}}}+T\|\partial_{1}^{2}\xi\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{1-\frac{1}{q_{+}},q_{+}}}\|\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\eta\|_{L_{t}^{2}W^{\max(\frac{1}{q_{+}},1-\frac{1}{q_{+}}),q_{+}}}

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{1}\xi\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{2-\frac{1}{q_{+}},q_{+}}}\|\partial_{1}\eta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{2-\frac{1}{q_{+}},q_{+}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(T\|\partial_{t}\xi\|_{L_{t}^{2}W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{-}},q_{-}}}+\|\xi\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{+}},q_{+}}}\right)\|\eta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{+}},q_{+}}}+T\|\xi\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{+}},q_{+}}}\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{L_{t}^{2}W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{-}},q_{-}}}.

For the boundary condition on
\Sigma_{s}
, we have

\left\|\int_{0}^{t}(S_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}^{n}}(q,v)\nu)\cdot\tau\right\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{1-\frac{1}{q_{+}},q_{+}}}\lesssim\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{-}},q_{-}}}(\|q\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{1,q_{+}}}+\|v\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{2,q_{+}}}).

For the kinematic boundary condition, by integration by part, we have

\displaystyle\|\int_{0}^{t}v\cdot\partial_{t}\mathcal{N}^{n}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{2-\frac{1}{q_{+}},q_{+}}}\lesssim\|\int_{0}^{t}\partial_{1}v\cdot\partial_{t}\mathcal{N}^{n}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{1-\frac{1}{q_{+}},q_{+}}}+\|\int_{0}^{t}\partial_{t}v\partial_{1}^{2}\eta^{n}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{1-\frac{1}{q_{+}},q_{+}}}

\displaystyle+\|v\partial_{1}^{2}\eta^{n}(0)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{1-\frac{1}{q_{+}},q_{+}}}+\|v\partial_{1}^{2}\eta^{n}(T)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{1-\frac{1}{q_{+}},q_{+}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|v\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{2,q_{+}}}\|\partial_{t}\eta^{n}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{-}},q_{-}}}+\|\partial_{t}v\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{2,q_{-}}}\|\eta^{n}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{+}},q_{+}}},

\displaystyle\|\int_{0}^{t}(u_{1}\partial_{t}\partial_{1}\xi)\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{2-\frac{1}{q_{+}},q_{+}}}\lesssim\|u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{2,q_{+}}}\|\xi\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{+}},q_{+}}}+\|u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{2-\frac{1}{q_{+}},q_{+}}(\Sigma)}\|\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi\|_{L_{t}^{2}W^{\max(\frac{1}{q_{+}},1-\frac{1}{q_{+}}),q_{+}}}

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{t}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}W^{\max(\frac{1}{q_{+}},1-\frac{1}{q_{+}}),q_{+}}(\Sigma)}\|\partial_{t}\xi\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{-}},q_{-}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{2,q_{+}}}\|\xi\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{+}},q_{+}}}+\|u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{2,q_{+}}}\|\partial_{t}\xi\|_{L_{t}^{2}W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{-}},q_{-}}}+\|\partial_{t}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}W^{2,q_{-}}}\|\xi\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{-}},q_{-}}}.

Finally, for the contact point condition, using the similar computation as for the capillary equation, we obtain

\left\|\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{R}_{z}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta)\partial_{t}\partial_{1}\xi(\pm\ell)\right\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}}\lesssim\|\xi\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{+}},q_{+}}}\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{L_{t}^{2}W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{-}},q_{-}}}.

Therefore, given that
\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{L_{t}^{2}W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{-}},q_{-}}}+\|\eta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{+}},q_{+}}}+\|u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{2,q_{+}}}+\|\partial_{t}u\|_{L_{t}^{2}W^{2,q_{-}}}\leq\delta\ll 1
. We obtain the estimate (
3.26
).
∎

4.
Construction of Strong Solutions to the Linear System

In this section, we aim to construct the solution to (
1.17
) under the assumptions that the forcing terms
F^{i}
are given and independent of the unknowns. To begin with, we derive a solution of (
1.28
) and then solve (
1.17
) by passing to the limit. To solve (
1.28
), we suppose
(u,\eta,p,v_{l},\xi_{l})
,
n
,
k
, as well as
\mathcal{A}(\eta^{n})
,
J(\eta^{n})
,
\mathcal{N}(\eta^{n})
, etc. are given.

4.1.
Galerkin Setup

Our goal is to use the Galerkin method to establish the existence and uniqueness of solutions to (
1.28
), in which the unknowns are
(v_{d},q_{d},\xi_{d})
and their derivatives. We omit the subscripts
(k,n)
when there is little chance of confusion. In order to utilize the Galerkin method, we must first construct a countable basis of
\mathcal{W}(t)
for each
t\in[0,T]
. It is evident that
\mathcal{W}_{\sigma}(t)\hookrightarrow\mathcal{H}_{\sigma}^{0}(t)\hookrightarrow(\mathcal{W}_{\sigma}(t))^{\ast}
.
Due to the time-dependent incompressible condition
u\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}\in H^{1}(\Sigma)
, the basis should also be time-dependent.

We note a key technical difficulty. We must ensure that the initial data for the sequence of Galerkin approximate solutions
(v_{d}^{m}(0),D_{t}v_{d}^{m}(0))
converge as
m\to\infty
to the initial data of the PDE (
1.17
) or equivalently (
1.28
). The initial data

(v_{d}(0),D_{t}v_{d}(0))=(v_{d0}^{n,k},D_{t}^{2}u(0))\in W^{2,q_{+}}(\Omega)\times H^{1}(\Omega),

are compatible with the dynamical energy. The definitions of the initial functions
v_{d0}^{n,k},D_{t}^{2}u(0)
are given in Appendix
B
and
D
.

However, the regularity
v_{d}(0)\in W^{2,q_{+}}(\Omega)
is not strong enough to ensure the following property

v_{d}(0)\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}(0)\in H^{1}(\Sigma),

which is required in the definition of space
\mathcal{W}(0)
as (
2.12
). Hence, it is not suitable to construct
v_{d}^{m}(0)
by directly projecting
v_{d}(0)
to some finite dimensional subspace of
\mathcal{W}
. This presents the primary technical difficulty in the Galerkin scheme.

In addition, both terms in bulk and terms on the boundary are contained in the Galerkin approximated weak formulation, as (
4.1
). Consequently, it is challenging to discretize both
v_{d}
and
\xi_{d}
simultaneously to deduce the convergence from

(v^{m}_{d}(0),D_{t}v^{m}_{d}(0),\xi_{d}^{m}(0),\partial_{t}\xi_{d}^{m}(0)),

to
(v_{d}(0),D_{t}v_{d}(0),\xi_{d}(0),\partial_{t}\xi_{d}(0))=(v_{d0}^{n,k},D_{t}^{2}u(0),\xi_{d0}^{n,k},\partial_{t}^{2}\eta(0))
.

Recalling the classical Galerkin method for solving Navier-Stokes equations (for instance, see
[
2
]
and
[
21
]
), we must choose a special basis for our Galerkin process, to cancel the influence resulting from the boundary terms. Our construction for the basis is much more subtle because of the time-dependence and the convergence of approximated initial data as stated above.

We plan to first construct a basis of
\mathcal{H}_{\sigma}^{0}(0)
and
\mathcal{W}_{\sigma}(0)
, then we derive the time-dependent basis. For this purpose,
we define the map
\mathcal{J}:\mathcal{W}_{\sigma}(0)\rightarrow\mathcal{W}^{\ast}_{\sigma}(0)
via

\left<\mathcal{J}v,w\right>_{\ast}=(v,w)_{\mathcal{W}},\quad\forall v,w\in\mathcal{W}(0)
.
By Riesz representation theorem,
\mathcal{J}
is an isometric isomorphism. This enables us to define
A=\mathcal{J}^{-1}:\mathcal{W}_{\sigma}(0)^{\ast}\rightarrow\mathcal{W}_{\sigma}(0)
so that for any
f\in\mathcal{W}_{\sigma}(0)^{\ast}
,
Af\in\mathcal{W}_{\sigma}(0)
is uniquely determined via

\left<f,w\right>_{\ast}=(Af,w)_{\mathcal{W}(0)},\quad\forall w\in\mathcal{W}_{\sigma}(0)
,
which is equivalent to saying that
Af=v
is a weak solution to
\mathcal{J}v=f
.

Lemma 4.1
.

The operator
A
restricted on
\mathcal{H}_{\sigma}^{0}(0)
is a compact, positive and self-adjoint operator. Thus, the eigenvalues of
A
are
\{\lambda_{i}\}_{i=1}^{\infty}
such that
0<\lambda_{1}\leq\lambda_{2}\leq\cdots

and

\lambda_{i}\to\infty
, as
i\to\infty
.
Finally, there exists an orthonormal basis
\{\psi_{i}\}_{i=1}^{\infty}
of
\mathcal{H}_{\sigma}^{0}(0)
, where
\psi_{i}\in\mathcal{W}_{\sigma}(0)
is an eigenfunction of
\mathcal{J}
corresponding to
\lambda_{i}
:

\mathcal{J}\psi_{i}=\lambda_{i}\psi_{i}.

Moreover,
\left\{\frac{\psi_{i}}{\sqrt{\lambda_{i}}}\right\}_{i=1}^{\infty}
is an orthonormal basis of
\mathcal{W}_{\sigma}(0)
.

Proof.

Note that
\mathcal{W}_{\sigma}(0)\subset\subset\mathcal{H}_{\sigma}^{0}(0)\hookrightarrow\mathcal{W}_{\sigma}^{\ast}(0)
.
The restriction
A|_{\mathcal{H}_{\sigma}^{0}(0)}:\mathcal{H}_{\sigma}^{0}(0)\rightarrow\mathcal{W}_{\sigma}(0)
implies that
A|_{\mathcal{H}_{\sigma}^{0}(0)}:\mathcal{H}_{\sigma}^{0}(0)\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{\sigma}^{0}(0)
is compact.

We claim that
A
is also a positive, self-adjoint operator. For
f,g\in\mathcal{H}^{0}(0)
, let
u=Af
and
v=Ag
. Then

(u,v)_{\mathcal{W}^{*}(0)}=(Af,v)_{\mathcal{W}(0)}=(f,v)_{\mathcal{H}^{0}(0)}=(f,Ag)_{\mathcal{H}^{0}(0)}
,
and

(v,u)_{\mathcal{W}(0)}=(Ag,u)_{\mathcal{W}(0)}=(g,u)_{\mathcal{H}^{0}(0)}=(g,Af)_{\mathcal{H}^{0}(0)}
.
By symmetry,
(u,v)_{\mathcal{W}(0)}=(v,u)_{\mathcal{W}(0)}
. Thus,

(f,Ag)_{\mathcal{H}^{0}(0)}=(g,Af)_{\mathcal{H}^{0}(0)}=(Af,g)_{\mathcal{H}^{0}(0)}
.
Also,
(Af,f)_{\mathcal{H}^{0}(0)}=(f,f)_{\mathcal{W}(0)}\geq 0
. Therefore, our claim is valid.

Consequently, by Riesz-Schauder theorem, there exists a complete orthonormal basis
\{\psi_{i}\}_{i=1}^{\infty}\subseteq\mathcal{H}_{\sigma}^{0}(0)
such that

A\psi_{i}=\mu_{i}\psi_{i}
, and
\mu_{i}\to 0
, as
i\to\infty
.
But

A\psi_{i}=\mu_{i}\psi_{i}\in\mathcal{W}_{\sigma}(0)
, for all
\psi_{i}\in\mathcal{H}_{\sigma}^{0}(0)
,
so

\{\psi_{i}\}_{i=1}^{\infty}\subseteq\mathcal{W}_{\sigma}(0)
,
and

\mu_{i}(\psi_{i},w)_{\mathcal{W}(0)}=(A\psi_{i},w)_{\mathcal{W}(0)}=(\psi_{i},w)_{\mathcal{H}^{0}(0)},\forall w\in\mathcal{W}(0)
.
Then we have

(\psi_{i},w)_{\mathcal{H}^{0}(0)}=\lambda_{i}(\psi_{i},w)_{\mathcal{W}(0)}
with
\lambda_{i}=\frac{1}{\mu_{i}}\to\infty
, as
k\to\infty
.
Thus,
(\psi_{i},\psi_{j})_{\mathcal{W}(0)}=\lambda_{i}(\psi_{i},\psi_{j})_{\mathcal{H}^{0}(0)}=\lambda_{i}\delta_{ji}
implies
\{\psi_{i}/\sqrt{\lambda_{i}}\}
is a complete orthonormal basis of
\mathcal{W}_{\sigma}(0)
.
∎

Let
\phi^{j}=M(0)^{-1}\psi_{j}
. By Lemma
2.3
and (
2.16
),
L^{2}(\Omega)
is isomorphic to
\mathcal{H}_{\sigma}^{0}(0)
, so
\{\phi^{j}\}_{j=1}^{\infty}
is a basis of
L^{2}(\Omega)
. Moreover,
{W}_{\sigma}(0)
is isomorphic to
\mathcal{W}_{\sigma}(t)
and
\{\frac{\phi^{j}}{\sqrt{\lambda_{j}}}\}_{j=1}^{\infty}
is a basis of
{W}_{\sigma}(t)
. With the above preparations, we construct the time-dependent basis in the following proposition.

Proposition 4.2
.

For each
i>0
,
\phi^{i}
is defined above. Then for each
t\geq 0
,
\{\omega^{j}(t)=M(t)\frac{\phi^{j}}{\sqrt{\lambda_{j}}}\}_{j=1}^{\infty}
is a basis of
\mathcal{W}_{\sigma}(t)
(defined by (
2.12
)) and
\sqrt{\lambda_{j}}\omega^{j}(t)
is a basis of
\mathcal{H}_{\sigma}^{0}(t)
.

Proof.

By the definition
\omega^{j}(t)=M(t)\frac{\phi^{j}}{\sqrt{\lambda_{j}}}
, we have
\omega^{j}(0)=M(0)\frac{\phi^{j}(0)}{\sqrt{\lambda_{j}}}=\frac{\psi_{j}}{\sqrt{\lambda_{j}}}
is an orthonormal basis of
\mathcal{W}_{\sigma}(0)
. We use Lemma
2.3
and (
2.16
) to deduce that

\{\omega^{j}(t)\}_{j=1}^{\infty}
is a basis of
\mathcal{W}_{\sigma}(t)
and
\sqrt{\lambda_{j}}\omega^{j}(t)
is a basis of
\mathcal{H}_{\sigma}^{0}(t)
.
∎

4.2.
Galerkin Approximation for Initial Data

We note that
\xi_{d}(0)
can be represented by the normal trace of
v_{d0}
due to the kinematic boundary condition. In the Galerkin method for
t>0
, we construct the approximate solutions
v_{d}^{m}
and
D_{t}v_{d}^{m}
and then pass to the limit to find
v_{d}
and
D_{t}v_{d}
, and require the initial data
(v_{d}^{m}(0),D_{t}v_{d}^{m}(0))
to converge to
(v_{d0}^{n,k}(0),D_{t}^{2}v(0))
. Specifically, we can prove that

Theorem 4.3
.

Suppose that
\{\omega^{j}(t)\}_{j=1}^{\infty}
is the basis defined as in Proposition
4.2
for the Hilbert space
\mathcal{W}_{\sigma}(t)
for each
t\geq 0
. When
t=0
,
\mathcal{W}_{\sigma}^{m}(0):=\operatorname{span}\{\omega^{1}(0),\ldots,\omega^{m}(0)\}
, the Galerkin approximated initial data
(v_{d}^{m}(0),D_{t}v_{d}^{m}(0))\in\mathcal{W}_{\sigma}^{m}(0)
obeys the estimates for any
n,k>0

\|D_{t}v_{d}^{m}(0)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}\lesssim\|D_{t}^{2}u(0)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}+\|v_{d0}^{n,k}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}},\ \|v_{d}^{m}(0)\|_{{}_{0}\mathcal{H}^{1}}\lesssim\|D_{t}^{2}u(0)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}+\|v_{d0}^{n,k}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}.

Moreover,
(v_{d}^{m}(0),D_{t}v_{d}^{m}(0))
converge to
(v_{d0}^{n,k}(0),D_{t}^{2}u(0))
weakly in
\mathcal{W}\times\mathcal{H}^{0}
.

This theorem follows directly from Proposition
4.2
and the subsequent analysis.

With the basis in Proposition
4.2
, we use the Galerkin method to construct the approximate solutions
D_{t}v_{d}^{m}(t)
and
v_{d}^{m}(t)
, under the space
\mathcal{W}_{\sigma}^{m}(t)=\text{span}\{\omega^{j}(t)\}_{j=1}^{m}
with
t\geq 0
. In order to do that, we need to construct the initial data
(D_{t}v_{d}^{m}(0),v_{d}^{m}(0))
, and prove that they are uniformly bounded.

We first construct the initial data for the approximated solution
v_{d}^{m}(0)
. Instead of projecting
v_{d}(0)
into the space
\mathcal{W}_{\sigma}^{m}(0)
, we introduce a novel scheme.

Suppose that
D_{t}v_{d}^{m}(0)
is the
L^{2}
projection of
D_{t}^{2}u(0)
onto the space
\mathcal{W}_{\sigma}^{m}(0)
. This implies that
D_{t}v_{d}^{m}(0)\rightarrow D_{t}v_{d}(0)
strongly in
H^{0}
.
Then we construct
v_{d}^{m}(0)
via the following equation

(4.1)

\displaystyle{}\begin{aligned} (D_{t}v_{d}^{m}(0),\psi)_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}+((v_{d}^{m}(0),\psi))+[(v_{d}^{m}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n})(0),\psi\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}]_{\ell}+(\xi_{d}(0),\psi\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n})_{1,\Sigma_{k}}=\mathscr{F}_{1}(\psi)-((R^{n}v_{d}^{m})(0),\psi)_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}\end{aligned}

for any
\psi\in\mathcal{W}^{m}
, where

(4.2)

\displaystyle{}\begin{aligned} \mathscr{F}_{1}(\psi)=\int_{\Omega}F^{1}\cdot\psi J^{n}-\int_{-\ell}^{\ell}F^{4}\cdot\psi-\int_{\Sigma_{s}}F^{5}(\psi\cdot\tau)J^{n}-[F^{7},\psi\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}]_{\ell}+((R^{n}(v_{0}^{n}),\psi))+(\partial_{t}(R^{n}v_{0}^{n}),\psi)_{\mathcal{H}^{0}(0)}.\end{aligned}

Assuming
v_{d}^{m}(0)=\sum_{k=1}^{m}d_{k}^{m}(0)\omega^{k}
, it satisfies the matrix equation induced by equation (
4.1
):

\mathcal{B}\vec{d}^{T}-\mathscr{R}\vec{d}=\vec{F}^{T}-\vec{\xi}^{T}-\vec{Y}^{T}

where
\vec{d}=(d_{1}(0),\ldots,d_{m}(0))
,
\vec{F}=(\mathscr{F}_{1}(\omega^{1}),\ldots,\mathscr{F}_{1}(\omega^{m}))
,
\vec{\xi}=((\xi_{d}(0),\omega^{1}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}),\ldots,(\xi_{d}(0),\omega^{m}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}))
,
\vec{Y}=(((D_{t}v_{d}^{m}(0)),\omega^{1})_{\mathcal{H}^{0}},\ldots,((D_{t}v_{d}^{m}(0)),\omega^{m})_{\mathcal{H}^{0}})
,
\mathscr{R}_{i,j}=(R^{n}\omega^{i},\omega^{j})_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}
.
\mathcal{B}
is an invertible matrix matrix with elements
\mathcal{B}_{i,j}=(\omega^{i}(0),\omega^{j}(0))_{{}_{0}\mathcal{H}^{1}}
. Using the smallness of
\|\mathscr{R}\|\lesssim\|\partial_{t}\eta(0)\|_{W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{-}},q_{-}}}
and a Picard iteration, we can solve for
d_{j}^{m}
.

Moreover, Subtracting the weak form equation for the initial condition from equation (
4.1
), we obtain:

(4.3)

\displaystyle{}\begin{aligned} ((D_{t}v_{d}^{m}(0))-D_{t}v_{d}(0),\psi)_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}+((v_{d}^{m}(0)-v_{d}(0),\psi))+[v_{d}^{m}(0)\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}-v_{d}(0)\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n},\psi\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}]_{\ell}&\\
+(R^{n}v_{d}^{m}(0)-R^{n}v_{d}(0),\psi)&=0\end{aligned}

for any
\psi\in\mathcal{W}^{m}
. Setting
\psi=v_{d}^{m}
in equation (
4.3
), we rewrite it as follows:

\displaystyle((D_{t}v_{d}^{m}(0)),v_{d}^{m}(0))_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}+((v_{d}^{m}(0),v_{d}^{m}(0)))+[v_{d}^{m}(0)\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n},v_{d}^{m}(0)\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}]_{\ell}

\displaystyle=((D_{t}v_{d}(0)),v_{d}^{m})_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}+((v_{d}(0),v_{d}^{m}(0)))+[v_{d}(0)\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n},v_{d}^{m}(0)\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}]_{\ell}-(R^{n}v_{d}^{m}(0)-R^{n}v_{d}(0),v_{d}^{m}(0)).

Using Cauchy’s inequality and the fact that
\|D_{t}v_{d}^{m}(0)\|_{H^{0}}\lesssim\|D_{t}v_{d}(0)\|
, we obtain the following uniform estimate for the initial data
v_{d}^{m}(0)
:

((v_{d}^{m}(0),v_{d}^{m}(0)))+[v_{d}^{m}(0)\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n},v_{d}^{m}(0)\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}]_{\ell}\leq\|\partial_{t}v_{d}(0)\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}+\frac{1}{2}\|v_{d}^{m}(0)\|^{2}_{{}_{0}\mathcal{H}^{1}}+2\|v_{d}(0)\|^{2}_{{}_{0}\mathcal{H}^{1}}+\|v_{d}(0)\|_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}^{2}.

Therefore, we derive the following inequality

(4.4)

\displaystyle{}((v_{d}^{m}(0),v_{d}^{m}(0)))+[v_{d}^{m}(0)\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n},v_{d}^{m}(0)\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}]_{\ell}\lesssim\|\partial_{t}v_{d}(0)\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}+\|v_{d}(0)\|^{2}_{{}_{0}\mathcal{H}^{1}},

which leads to the following weak convergence theorem.

Proposition 4.4
.

It holds that
v_{d}^{m}(0)
converges to
v_{d}(0)
weakly in
{}_{0}\mathcal{H}^{1}
as
m\to\infty
.

Proof.

The uniform bound for
v_{d}^{m}(0)
in (
4.4
) allows us to extract a weakly convergent subsequence, still denote
v_{d}^{m}(0)
, such that

v_{d}^{m}(0)\rightharpoonup w\quad\text{in}~~{}_{0}\mathcal{H}^{1}
,
for some
w\in{}_{0}\mathcal{H}^{1}
, which implies that
\operatorname{div}_{\mathcal{A}^{n}}w=0
.

Passing to the limit
m\to\infty
in (
4.4
) and
using the fact that
(D_{t}v_{d}^{m}(0))\to D_{t}v_{d}(0)
in
\mathcal{H}^{0}
,
we obtain the following equation

(4.5)

(w-v_{d}(0),\psi)_{{}_{0}\mathcal{H}^{1}}+(R^{n}w-R^{n}v_{d}(0),\psi)_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}=0

for any
\psi\in\mathcal{W}(0)
. We now pull back to the initial state of the original deformable domain
\Omega(0)
by the inverse mapping
\Phi(0)^{-1}
and let

\tilde{w}\circ\Phi(0)=w,\ \widetilde{v_{d}}(0)\circ\Phi(0)=v_{d}(0),

so that
\tilde{w}\in L^{2}(\Omega(0)):=\{u\in L^{2}(\Omega(0))\}
and

\widetilde{v_{d}}(0)\in{}_{0}{H}^{1}(\Omega(0)):=\{u\in L^{2}(\Omega(0)):\|u\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}(\Omega(0))}^{2}+[u\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}]_{\ell}^{2}<\infty\}
.
We choose
\psi
in the closure of
C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)
under
\mathcal{W}
norm, that is a subset of
\mathcal{W}_{\sigma}
, so that (
4.3
) is reduced to

(\tilde{w}-\widetilde{v_{d}}(0),\psi)_{H^{1}(\Omega(0))}+(R^{n}\tilde{w}-R^{n}\tilde{v_{d}}(0),\psi)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}=0.

Due to the smallness of
\|R^{n}(0)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\lesssim\|\partial_{t}\eta^{n}(0)\|_{W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{-}},q_{-}}}
, the inner product

(\phi,\psi)_{H^{1}(\Omega(0))}+(R^{n}\phi,\psi)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}
is positive when
\phi=\psi
. Since
C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)
is dense in
H^{1}(\Omega)
, we have

\tilde{w}-\widetilde{v_{d}}(0)=0
and
w=v_{d}(0)
by
\Phi(0)^{-1}
.
∎

4.3.
Construction of Strong Solutions

Suppose that all the forcing terms
F^{i}
are in the spaces satisfying estimate (
4.9
). We say that

(v_{d},q_{d},\xi_{d})\in L^{2}W^{2,q_{+}}\times L^{2}W^{1,q_{+}}\times L^{2}W^{3-1/q_{+},\,q_{+}}

is a strong solution to (
1.17
) if, for each
j=0,1,2
,
\partial_{t}^{j}v\in L^{\infty}H^{0}\cap L^{2}H^{1}
and
\partial_{t}^{j}\xi\in L^{\infty}H^{1}\cap L^{2}H^{3/2-\alpha}
,
and if
(v_{d},q_{d},\xi_{d})
satisfies (
1.17
).

In the following theorem, we construct strong solutions to (
1.17
) by first solving (
1.28
) via the Galerkin method. For fixed
n
,
k
and prescribed functions
(v_{l},\xi_{l})
, the construction proceeds in four main steps:

•

Step 1. Using a basis of
\mathcal{W}_{\sigma}(t)
and ODE theory, we construct the Galerkin solutions
(v_{d}^{m},\xi_{d}^{m})
to (
1.28
) in an
m
-dimensional subspace.

•

Step 2. We differentiate the system in time to derive equations for
(D_{t}v_{d}^{m},\partial_{t}\xi_{d}^{m})
. Energy estimates yield uniform bounds for these quantities. Passing to the limit as
m\to\infty
using functional analytic arguments, we recover the weak formulation and improve the regularity of
(\xi_{d},\partial_{t}\xi_{d})
.

•

Step 3. We recover the pressure using Theorem
3.6
. Furthermore, Theorem
3.5
implies that
(v_{d},\xi_{d})
is in fact a strong solution to (
1.28
).

•

Step 4. We derive uniform bounds for the solutions of (
1.28
) that are independent of
k
.

Once the solution to (
1.28
) and the corresponding
k
-independent uniform estimates are established, we pass the limit as
k\to+\infty
. We then apply the contraction mapping theorem to derive a fixed point satisfying
(v_{l},\xi_{l})=(v,\xi)
, which yields a solution to (
1.33
).

It then remains to pass to the limit
\eta^{n}\to\eta
. To this end, we first invoke Theorem
3.7
to establish a
q_{+}
-elliptic estimate for the zeroth-order quantities
(v,q,\xi)
. We then apply the a priori estimates developed in
[
10
]
to derive uniform bounds independent of
n
, which enable us to pass to the limit
n\to\infty
and thereby complete the proof

Before constructing the solution, we first introduce the following definitions:

(4.6)

\displaystyle\mathscr{D}(u,p,\eta)

\displaystyle:=\Big(\|u\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|p\|_{L^{2}W^{1,q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\eta\|_{L^{2}W^{3-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}\Big)+\sum_{j=0}^{2}\Big(\|\partial_{t}^{j}u\|_{L^{2}H^{1}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{j}u\|_{L^{2}L^{2}(\Sigma_{s})}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=0}^{2}\Big(\|\partial_{t}^{j}\eta\|_{L^{2}H^{3/2-\alpha}}^{2}+\|[\partial_{t}^{j}u\cdot\mathcal{N}]_{\ell}^{2}\|_{L_{t}^{2}}\Big)+\|\partial_{t}^{3}\eta\|_{L^{2}H^{1/2-\alpha}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\Big(\|\partial_{t}u\|_{L^{2}W^{2,q_{-}}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}p\|_{L^{2}W^{1,q_{-}}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{L^{2}W^{3-1/q_{-},q_{-}}}^{2}\Big),

(4.7)

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}(u,p,\eta)

\displaystyle:=\|u\|_{L^{\infty}W^{2,q_{+}}}^{2}+\sum_{i=0}^{1}\|\partial_{t}^{i}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H^{1+\varepsilon_{-}/2}}^{2}+\sum_{k=0}^{2}\|\partial_{t}^{k}u\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L^{2}}^{2}+\|p\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{1,q_{-}}}^{2}+\sum_{i=0}^{1}\|\partial_{t}^{i}p\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H^{0}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{t}^{i}\eta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}W^{3-1/q_{-},q_{-}}}^{2}+\sum_{i=0}^{1}\|\partial_{t}^{i}\eta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H^{3/2+(\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha)/2}}^{2}+\sum_{j=0}^{2}\Big(\|\partial_{t}^{j}\eta\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H^{1}}^{2}+\|[\partial_{t}^{j}u\cdot\mathcal{N}]_{\ell}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}}^{2}\Big),

(4.8)

\mathfrak{K}(u,p,\eta):=\mathscr{E}(u,p,\eta)+\mathscr{D}(u,p,\eta).

Compared to (
1.10
) and (
1.11
), we add only regularity in time here.

Now, we state our main theorem in this section as follows.

Theorem 4.5
.

Assume that the initial data are constructed in Section
B.1
.
Suppose that
\mathfrak{K}(u,\eta,p)\leq\delta
(as defined in (
4.8
)) is smaller than the constant
\delta_{0}
defined in Lemma
3.3
, Lemma
2.3
and Theorem 4.7 in
[
10
]
.
Then there exists a unique strong solution
(v_{d},q_{d},\xi_{d},v,q,\xi)
solving (
1.17
) and (
1.18
). Moreover, the solution obeys the estimate

(4.9)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathfrak{K}(v,q,\xi)&\lesssim\exp\big\{T(\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{L^{\infty}H^{3/2+(\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha)/2}}+\|\eta\|_{L^{\infty}W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{-}},q_{-}}})\big\}\\
&\quad\quad\bigg\{\mathcal{E}(u_{0},p_{0},\xi_{0})+\|(F^{1}-F^{4}-F^{5})(0)\|^{2}_{(\mathcal{H}^{1})^{\ast}}+\mathfrak{F}+\mathcal{Z}+\mathfrak{K}(u,p,\eta)\bigg\},\end{aligned}

where

(4.10)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathfrak{F}=\big(1+\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{L^{\infty}H^{3/2+\varepsilon_{-}/2}}^{2}\big)\Big(\|F^{1}\|_{L^{2}L^{q_{-}}}^{2}+\|F^{4}\|_{L^{2}W^{1-1/q_{-},q_{-}}}^{2}+\|F^{5}\|_{L^{2}W^{1-1/q_{-},q_{-}}}^{2}\Big)\\
+\big(1+\|\partial_{t}\eta\|_{L^{\infty}H^{3/2+\varepsilon_{-}/2}}^{2}\big)\|\partial_{t}(F^{1}-F^{4}-F^{5})\|_{(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{T})^{\ast}}^{2}+\sum_{j=0}^{1}\|[F^{7,j}]_{\ell}\|_{L^{2}}^{2},\end{aligned}

and

(4.11)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}=\|\int_{0}^{t}F^{1}\|_{L^{\infty}L^{q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\int_{0}^{t}F^{4}\|_{L^{\infty}W^{1-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\int_{0}^{t}F^{5}\|_{L^{\infty}W^{1-1/q_{+},q_{+}}}^{2}+\|\int_{0}^{t}[F^{7}]_{\ell}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}}^{2}

where ”
\lesssim
” omits a constant depending on
(g,\sigma,\ell,|\Omega|)
.

Proof.

Step 1 – Galerkin Setup

We begin by constructing the solution to (
1.28
) for any given
n,k
and prescribed functions
(u,\eta,p,\eta^{k},u^{k})
,
(\eta^{n},v_{l}^{k},\xi_{l}^{k})
.

In order to utilize the Galerkin method, we choose the same basis
\{\omega^{j}(t)\}_{j=1}^{\infty}
of
{}\mathcal{W}_{\sigma}
constructed in Proposition
4.2
for each
t\in[0,T]
, that satisfies the time-dependent requirement of condition
u\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}\in H^{1}(-\ell,\ell)
. Furthermore, tthe time derivatives of these basis vectors are expressed as a finite linear combination of these basis vectors. In particular,
\partial_{t}\omega^{j}=R^{n}\omega^{j}
,
where
R(t)
is defined by
R^{n}(t):=\partial_{t}M^{n}(t)(M^{n})^{-1}(t)
.
For any integer
m\geq 1
, we define the finite dimensional space

\mathcal{W}_{\sigma}^{m}(t):=\text{span}\{\omega^{1}(t),\cdots,\omega^{m}(t)\}\subseteq\mathcal{W}_{\sigma}(t)
,
and we write

\mathcal{P}^{m}_{t}:\mathcal{W}_{\sigma}(t)\rightarrow\mathcal{W}_{\sigma}^{m}(t)

for the
\mathcal{W}
orthogonal projection onto
\mathcal{W}^{m}(t)
. Clearly, for each
\omega\in\mathcal{W}_{\sigma}(t)
,
\mathcal{P}^{m}_{t}\omega\rightarrow\omega
as
m\rightarrow\infty
.

Step 2 – Solving the Approximate Problem

In this step, we fix
n,k
and the given functions
v_{l},\xi_{l}
.

For each
m\geq 1
, we define an approximate solution
v_{d}^{m}(t):=d^{m}_{j}(t)\omega^{j}(t)
, with
d^{m}_{j}:[0,T]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}\ \text{for}\ j=1,\dots,m
,
where, as usual, we use the Einstein convention of summation of the repeated index
j
. We define
\xi_{d}^{m}
similarly via the following transport equation:

\partial_{t}^{2}\xi_{d}^{m}=D_{t}v_{d}^{m}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}+D_{t}(R^{n}v_{l}^{k})\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}+u_{1}^{k}\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi_{d}^{m}+\partial_{t}u_{1}^{k}\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi_{l}^{k}.

This system admits the following solution:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\xi_{d}^{m}(s,X(s,x))=

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\xi_{d}(0)(x)+\int_{0}^{t}((D_{t}v_{d}^{m})\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n})(y,X(y,x))\mathrm{d}y

(4.12)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}(\partial_{t}u_{1}^{k}\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi_{l}^{k}+D_{t}(R^{n}v_{l}^{k})\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}))(y,X(y,x))\mathrm{d}y,

where
X
satisfies the following ODE:

\frac{d}{dt}X(t,x)=u_{1}^{k}(t,x)~~~\operatorname{and}~~~X(0,x)=x.

Then the equation (
4.12
) is equivalent to:

(4.13)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \partial_{t}\xi_{d}^{m}(s,x)=&\partial_{t}\xi_{d}(0)(X(-s,x))+\int_{0}^{s}((D_{t}v_{d}^{m})\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n})(y,X(y,X(-s,x)))\mathrm{d}y\\
&+\int_{0}^{s}(\partial_{t}u_{1}^{k}\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi_{l}^{k}+D_{t}(R^{n}v_{l}^{k})\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n})(y,X(y,X(-s,x)))\mathrm{d}y.\end{aligned}

Using the fact that

\frac{d}{dt}f(t,X(t,X(-s,x)))=\partial_{t}f-u_{1}^{k}\partial_{x}f,

integrating equation (
4.13
) from
0
to
t
, and using integration by part, we obtain the following equation for
\xi_{d}^{m}

\displaystyle{}\xi_{d}^{m}(t,x)=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\partial_{t}^{2}\eta_{0}(X(-s,x))ds+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s}(\partial_{t}u_{1}^{k}\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi_{l}^{k}+D_{t}(R^{n}v_{l}^{k})\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n})(y,X(y,X(-s,x)))\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}s

(4.14)

\displaystyle+\xi_{d}(0,x)-\int_{0}^{t}((v_{d}^{m})\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n})(0,X(-s,x))\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}((v_{d}^{m})\cdot\mathcal{N}^{{}^{n}})(s,x)\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s}u_{1}^{k}\partial_{1}((v_{d}^{m})\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n})(y,X(y,X(-s,x)))\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}s,

where
v_{d}^{m}(s)
and
u_{1}(t,x)
denote their traces on
\Sigma
. Using the fact that
u_{1}(\pm\ell)=0
and
W^{2,q_{-}}(\Omega)\hookrightarrow C^{0}(-\ell,\ell)
, it follows that
X(t,x)
is well defined and

X(t,\pm\ell)=\pm\ell~\operatorname{for}~\operatorname{any}~t\geq 0,

ensuring that the flow
X
induced by
u_{1}
fixes the boundary.

We choose the coefficients
d^{m}_{j}(t)
so that

(4.15)

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}v_{d}^{m},w)_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}+((v_{d}^{m},w))+(\xi_{d}^{m},w\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n})_{1,\Sigma_{0}}+(\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{R}_{z}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta^{k})\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi_{d}^{m},\partial_{1}(w\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}))_{L^{2}}+[v_{d}^{m}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n},w\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}]_{\ell}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}F^{1}\cdot wJ^{n}+\int_{-\ell}^{\ell}F^{4}\cdot w-\int_{\Sigma_{s}}F^{5}(w\cdot\tau)J^{n}-[F^{7},w\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}]_{\ell}+((R^{n}D_{t}v_{l},w))+(\partial_{t}(R^{n}v_{l}),w)_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}

\displaystyle+\left(\int_{0}^{t}\partial_{1}(\mathcal{R}_{zz}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta^{k})\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi_{l}^{k}\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\eta^{k}),(w\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n})\right)_{L^{2}}+[\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{R}_{zz}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta^{k})\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi_{l}^{k}\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\eta^{k},w\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}]_{\ell},

for each
w\in\mathcal{W}^{m}(t)
.
From equation (
4.14
), we may compute

\displaystyle(\xi_{d}^{m}(t,x),w\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}(t))_{1,\Sigma_{0}}=\left(\int_{0}^{t}\partial_{t}^{2}\eta_{0}(X(-s,x))ds+2\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s}(\partial_{t}u_{1}^{k}\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi_{l}^{k})(y,X(y,X(-s,x))dy\,ds,w\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}(t)\right)_{1,\Sigma_{0}}

\displaystyle+(\xi_{d}(0),w\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}(t))_{1,\Sigma_{0}}-\left(\int_{0}^{t}\Big[(v_{d}^{m}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n})(0,X(-s,x))+(v_{d}^{m}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n})(s,x)\Big]ds,w\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}(t)\right)_{1,\Sigma_{0}}

\displaystyle-\left(\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s}\Big[u_{1}^{k}\partial_{1}((v_{d}^{m}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n})(y,X(y,X(-s,x)))-2(D_{t}(R^{n}v_{l}^{k})\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n})(y,X(y,X(-s,x)))\Big]dy\,ds,w\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}(t)\right)_{1,\Sigma_{0}},

and

\displaystyle\left(\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{R}_{z}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta^{k})\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi_{d},\partial_{1}(w\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n})\right)_{L^{2}}=\left(\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{R}_{z}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta^{k})\partial_{t}\partial_{1}\xi_{d}(0)(X(-s,x)),\partial_{1}(w\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n})\right)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+2(\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{R}_{z}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta^{k})\int_{0}^{s}\partial_{1}(\partial_{t}u_{1}^{k}\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi_{l}^{k})(y,X(y,X(-s,x))),\partial_{1}(w\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}))_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\left(\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{R}_{z}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta^{k})\big[-\partial_{1}(v_{d}^{m}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n})(0,X(-s,x))+\partial_{1}(v_{d}^{m}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n})(s,x)\big],\partial_{1}(w\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n})\right)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\left(\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s}\mathcal{R}_{z}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta^{k})\big[\partial_{1}(D_{t}(R^{n}v_{l}^{k})\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n})-\partial_{1}(u_{1}^{k}\partial_{1}(v_{d}^{m}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}))(y,X(y,X(-s,x)))\big],\partial_{1}(w\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n})\right)_{L^{2}}.

Substituting these two equations to (
4.15
), this equation is equivalent to the equation of
d^{m}_{j}
given by

(4.16)

\displaystyle\dot{d}^{m}_{i}(\omega^{i},\omega^{j})_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}+d^{m}_{i}\tilde{\mathfrak{B}}-\int_{0}^{t}d_{i}^{m}(s)\tilde{\mathfrak{C}}(t,s)\,\mathrm{d}s-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s}d_{i}^{m}(y)\tilde{\mathfrak{J}}(t,s,y)dy\,ds=\tilde{\mathfrak{F}}(t),

for
i,j=1,\dots,m
, where

\tilde{\mathfrak{B}}(t)=(R^{n}(t)\omega^{i},\omega^{j})_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}+((\omega^{i},\omega^{j}))+[\omega^{i}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n},\omega^{j}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}]_{\ell}

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathfrak{C}}(t,s)

\displaystyle=\Big((\omega^{i}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n})(0,X(-s,x))+(\omega^{i}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n})(s,x),\omega^{j}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}(t)\Big)_{1,\Sigma_{0}}

\displaystyle+\Big(\mathcal{R}_{z}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta^{k})\omega^{i}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}(0,X(-s,x))+\mathcal{R}_{z}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta^{k})\omega^{i}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}(s,x)ds,\partial_{1}(\omega^{j}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n})\Big)_{L^{2}},

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathfrak{J}}(t,s,y)

\displaystyle=(u_{1}^{k}\partial_{1}((v_{d}^{m})\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n})(y,X(y,X(-s,x))),\omega^{j}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}(t))_{1,\Sigma_{0}}

\displaystyle-\Big(\mathcal{R}_{z}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta^{k})\partial_{1}(u_{1}^{k}\partial_{1}(\omega^{i}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}(y,X(y,X(-s,x))))),\partial_{1}(\omega^{j}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n})\Big)_{L^{2}},

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathfrak{F}}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}F^{1}\cdot\omega^{j}J^{n}-\int_{-\ell}^{\ell}F^{4}\cdot\omega^{j}-\int_{\Sigma_{s}}F^{5}(\omega^{j}\cdot\tau)J^{n}-(\eta_{0},\omega^{j}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}(t))_{1,\Sigma_{k}}-[F^{7},\omega^{j}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}]_{\ell}

\displaystyle-(\xi_{d}(0),\omega^{j}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n})_{1,\Sigma_{n}}-\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\partial_{t}^{2}\eta_{0}(X(-s,x))\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{s}(\partial_{1}(\partial_{t}u_{1}^{k}\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi_{l}^{k})(y,X(y,X(-s,x)))\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}s,\omega^{i}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}\Big)_{1,\Sigma_{k}}

\displaystyle+\left(\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s}\mathcal{R}_{z}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta^{k})\partial_{1}(D_{t}(R^{n}D_{t}v_{l}^{k})\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}))(y,X(y,X(-s,x)))\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}s,\partial_{1}(\omega^{j}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n})\right)_{L^{2}}+((R^{n}D_{t}v_{l},\omega^{j}))

\displaystyle+(\partial_{t}(R^{n}v_{l}),\omega^{j})+\left(\int_{0}^{t}\partial_{1}(\mathcal{R}_{zz}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta^{k})\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi_{l}^{k}\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\eta^{k})\mathrm{d}s,(\omega^{j}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n})\right)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\left[\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{R}_{zz}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta^{k})\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi_{l}^{k}\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\eta^{k}\mathrm{d}s,\omega^{j}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}\right]_{\ell}.

Since
\{\omega^{j}(t)\}_{j=1}^{\infty}
is a basis of
\mathcal{W}_{\sigma}(t)
, the
m\times m
matrix with
i,j
entry
(\omega^{i},\omega^{j})_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}
is invertible. Thus the initial data
d^{m}_{i}(0)
are determined uniquely via
d^{m}_{i}(0)(\omega^{i}(0),\omega^{j}(0))_{\mathcal{W}}=(\mathcal{P}_{0}^{m}u_{0},\omega^{j}(0))_{\mathcal{W}}
.
Then we view (
4.16
) as an integro-differentia system of the form

(4.17)

\dot{d}^{m}(t)+\mathfrak{B}(t)d^{m}(t)+\int_{0}^{t}\mathfrak{C}(t,s)d^{m}(s)\,\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{s}\mathfrak{J}(t,s,y)d^{m}(y)dyds=\tilde{\mathfrak{F}}(t).

The
m\times m
matrix
\mathfrak{B}
belongs to
C^{1}([0,T])
and
\mathfrak{C}(t,s)
belongs to
C^{1}(D)
, where
D=\{(t,s)|0\leq s\leq t\leq T\}
, and
\mathfrak{I}
belongs to
L^{2}(Q)
, where
Q=\{(t,s,y)|0\leq y\leq s\leq t\}
and the forcing term
\tilde{\mathfrak{F}}\in H^{1}([0,T])
. By the linear theory of Volterra integro-differential equations developed by S. Grossman and R. Miller
[
7
]
, or
[
24
]
, directly using Picard iteration, (
4.17
) has a unique solution
d^{m}\in C^{2}([0,T])
achieving the initial data
d^{m}(0)
.

Step 3 – Energy Estimate for
D_{t}v_{d}^{m}

In this step, we fix
n,k
and given functions
v_{l},\xi_{l}
, and construct a uniform bound for
(v_{d}^{m},\xi_{d}^{m})
independent of
m
.

Because the system’s kinematic boundary condition is quasilinear, we must first derive the energy estimate for
(D_{t}v_{d}^{m},\partial_{t}\xi_{d}^{m})
.

Suppose that
w=b_{j}^{m}\omega^{j}\in\mathcal{W}^{m}(t)
for
b_{j}^{m}\in C^{2}([0,T])
. From the definition of
\mathcal{W}_{\sigma}(t)
,
\partial_{t}w
may not be a function in
\mathcal{W}_{\sigma}^{m}(t)
since the basis
w^{j}
depends on time
t
. Therefore, we cannot establish the second-order system by simply taking a time derivative of the first-order equation.

Fortunately,
\partial_{t}w(t)-R^{n}(t)w(t)\in\mathcal{W}_{\sigma}^{m}(t)
provided that
w(t)\in\mathcal{W}_{\sigma}^{m}(t)
. From the definition of
\mathcal{W}_{\sigma}(t)
, we now use this
w
in (
4.15
), differentiate the resulting equation with respect to time, and then subtract this from the equation (
4.15
) with the test function
D_{t}w=\partial_{t}w-R^{n}w
where
R^{n}
is defined in Proposition
2.5
. This eliminates the
\partial_{t}w
terms and leaves us with the following equation

(4.18)

\displaystyle\left(\partial_{t}D_{t}v_{d}^{m},w\right)_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}+(\partial_{t}(R^{n}v_{d}^{m}),w)_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}+(D_{t}v_{d}^{m}+R^{n}v_{d}^{m},R^{n}w)_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}+((D_{t}v_{d}^{m}+R^{n}v_{d}^{m},w))+((v_{d}^{m},R^{n}w))

\displaystyle+(\partial_{t}\xi_{d}^{m},w\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n})_{1,\Sigma_{k}}+(\xi_{d}^{m},R^{n}w\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n})_{1,\Sigma_{k}}+(\xi_{d}^{m},w\cdot\partial_{t}\mathcal{N}^{n})_{1,\Sigma_{k}}+[\partial_{t}v_{d}^{m}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n},w\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}]_{\ell}+[v_{d}^{m}\cdot\partial_{t}\mathcal{N}^{n},w\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}]_{\ell}

\displaystyle+[v_{d}^{m}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n},R^{n}w\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}]_{\ell}+[v_{d}^{m},w\cdot\partial_{t}\mathcal{N}^{n}]_{\ell}+(\partial_{t}v_{d}^{m},w\partial_{t}J^{n})_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\beta(D_{t}v_{d}^{m}\cdot\tau,(w\cdot\tau)\partial_{t}J^{n})_{L^{2}(\Sigma_{s})}

\displaystyle+\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{R}_{z}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta^{k})\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi_{d},\partial_{1}(w\cdot\partial_{t}\mathcal{N}^{n})\Big)_{L^{2}}+\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{R}_{z}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta^{k})\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi_{d},\partial_{1}(R^{n}w\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n})\Big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\Big(\partial_{1}(\mathcal{R}_{zz}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta^{k})\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi_{l}^{k}\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\eta^{k}),(w\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n})\Big)_{L^{2}}+[\mathcal{R}_{zz}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta^{k})\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi_{l}^{k}\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\eta^{k},w\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}]_{\ell}+(\partial_{t}^{2}(R^{n}v_{l}),w)_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}

\displaystyle+\Big(\partial_{t}(R^{n}v_{l}),R^{n}w)_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}+((R^{n}v_{l}),w\partial_{t}J^{n}(J^{n})^{-1}\Big)_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}\mathcal{F}(w)-\mathcal{F}(\partial_{t}w)+\mathcal{F}(R^{n}w)+((\partial_{t}(R^{n}v_{l}),w))+((R^{n}D_{t}v_{l},R^{n}w))

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega}\frac{\mu}{2}\mathbb{D}_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}^{n}}v_{d}^{m}:\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{A}^{n}}wJ^{n}-\int_{\Omega}\frac{\mu}{2}J^{n}\Big(\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{A}^{n}}v_{d}^{m}:\mathbb{D}_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}^{n}}w+\partial_{t}J^{n}K^{n}\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{A}^{n}}v_{d}^{m}:\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{A}^{n}}w\Big)

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega}\frac{\mu}{2}\mathbb{D}_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}^{n}}(R^{n}v_{l}):\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{A}^{n}}wJ^{n}-\int_{\Omega}\frac{\mu}{2}J^{n}\Big(\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{A}^{n}}(R^{n}v_{l}):\mathbb{D}_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}^{n}}w+\partial_{t}J^{n}K^{n}\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{A}^{n}}(R^{n}v_{l}):\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{A}^{n}}w\Big),

where for brevity we have written

(4.19)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(w)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}F^{1}\cdot wJ^{n}+\int_{-\ell}^{\ell}F^{4}\cdot w-\int_{\Sigma_{s}}F^{5}(w\cdot\tau)J^{n}-[F^{7},w\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}]_{\ell}.

We first consider the terms on the boundary
\Sigma
and the contact points. According to
[
23
, Lemma A.1]
, we know that
(R^{n})^{T}\mathcal{N}^{n}=-\partial_{t}\mathcal{N}^{n}
. Then it holds that

(\xi_{d}^{m},R^{n}w\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n})_{1,\Sigma_{k}}+(\xi_{d}^{m},w\cdot\partial_{t}\mathcal{N}^{n})_{1,\Sigma_{k}}+[v_{d}^{m}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n},R^{n}w\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}]_{\ell}+[v_{d}^{m}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n},w\cdot\partial_{t}\mathcal{N}^{n}]_{\ell}=0,

\left(\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{R}_{z}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta^{k})\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi_{d},\partial_{1}(w\cdot\partial_{t}\mathcal{N}^{n})\right)_{L^{2}}+\left(\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{R}_{z}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta^{k})\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi_{d},\partial_{1}(R^{n}w\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n})\right)_{L^{2}}=0.

Let the test function be
w=D_{t}v_{d}^{m}
. We have the following relation.

[\partial_{t}v_{d}^{m}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n},D_{t}v_{d}^{m}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}]_{\ell}=[D_{t}v_{d}^{m}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}]^{2}_{\ell}+[D_{t}v_{d}^{m}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n},R^{n}v_{d}^{m}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}]_{\ell}.

The second term in the equation above vanishes since
R^{n}v_{d}^{m}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}(\pm\ell)=-v_{d}^{m}\cdot\partial_{t}\mathcal{N}^{n}(\pm\ell)=0
. Moreover, by the same reason, we have

(4.20)

[\partial_{t}v_{d}^{m}\cdot\partial_{t}\mathcal{N}^{n},w\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}]_{\ell}=[D_{t}v_{d}^{m}\cdot\partial_{t}\mathcal{N}^{n},w\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}]_{\ell}+[R^{n}v_{d}^{m}\cdot\partial_{t}\mathcal{N}^{n},w\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}]=0,

where we used the fact that
v_{d}^{m}
and
D_{t}v_{d}^{m}
are functions in
\mathcal{W}_{\sigma}(t)
, which implies that
(D_{t}v_{d}^{m}\cdot\partial_{t}\mathcal{N}^{n})(\pm\ell)=0
, and
R^{n}v_{d}^{m}\cdot\partial_{t}\mathcal{N}^{n}(\pm\ell)=v_{d}^{m}\cdot\partial_{t}\mathcal{N}^{n}(\pm\ell)=0
.

For the term in
(\cdot)_{1,\Sigma}
, using the kinematic boundary condition

\partial_{t}^{2}\xi_{d}^{m}=D_{t}(v_{d}^{m})\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}+D_{t}(R^{n}v_{l}^{k})\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}-\partial_{t}u_{1}^{k}\partial_{t}\partial_{1}\xi_{l}^{k}+u_{1}^{k}\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi_{d}^{m},

we obtain

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}\xi_{d}^{m},D_{t}v_{d}^{m}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n})_{1,\Sigma_{k}}=(\partial_{t}\xi_{d}^{m},\partial_{t}^{2}\xi_{d}^{m})_{1,\Sigma_{k}}+(\partial_{t}\xi_{d}^{m},\partial_{t}u_{1}^{k}\partial_{t}\partial_{1}\xi_{l}^{k})_{1,\Sigma_{k}}-(\partial_{t}\xi_{d}^{m},u_{1}^{n}\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi_{d}^{m})_{1,\Sigma_{k}}

\displaystyle-(\partial_{t}\xi_{d}^{m},D_{t}(R^{n}v_{l}^{k})\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n})_{1,\Sigma_{k}}=\frac{d}{dt}\frac{1}{2}\|\partial_{t}\xi_{d}^{m}\|_{1,\Sigma_{k}}^{2}-\int_{-\ell}^{\ell}\mathcal{R}_{zz}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta^{k})\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\eta^{k}(\partial_{t}\partial_{1}\xi_{d}^{m})^{2}

\displaystyle+(\partial_{t}\xi_{d}^{m},\partial_{t}u_{1}^{k}\partial_{t}\partial_{1}\xi_{l}^{k})_{1,\Sigma_{k}}-(\partial_{t}\xi_{d}^{m},u_{1}^{k}\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi_{d}^{m})_{1,\Sigma_{k}}-(\partial_{t}\xi_{d}^{m},D_{t}(R^{n}v_{l}^{k})\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n})_{1,\Sigma_{k}},

where we recall the definition of
(\cdot)_{1,\Sigma_{k}}
inner product as follows

(\partial_{t}\xi_{d},\partial_{t}^{2}\xi_{d})_{1,\Sigma_{k}}=\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}||\partial_{t}\xi_{d}||_{1,\Sigma_{k}}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\sigma\int_{-\ell}^{\ell}\mathcal{R}_{zz}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta^{k})\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\eta^{k}(\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi_{d})^{2}.

Applying all the computations above to equation (
4.18
), setting the test function to
w=D_{t}v_{d}^{m}
, and using integration by parts, we deduce

(\partial_{t}D_{t}v_{d}^{m},D_{t}v_{d}^{m})_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}=\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}||D_{t}v_{d}^{m}||^{2}_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}-\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}J^{n}(D_{t}v_{d}^{m})^{2}.

Thus, equation (
4.18
) can be rewritten as

(4.21)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left(\frac{1}{2}\|D_{t}v_{d}^{m}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\|\partial_{t}\xi_{d}^{m}\|_{1,\Sigma_{k}}^{2}\right)+\|D_{t}v_{d}^{m}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}^{2}+[D_{t}v_{d}^{m}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}]_{\ell}^{2}=I+II+III+IV,

where

\displaystyle I=

\displaystyle-\beta(\partial_{t}v_{d}^{m}\cdot\tau,(\partial_{t}v_{d}^{m}\cdot\tau)\partial_{t}J^{n})_{L^{2}(\Sigma_{s})}-(\partial_{t}\xi_{d}^{m},\partial_{t}u_{1}^{k}\partial_{t}\partial_{1}\xi_{l}^{k})_{1,\Sigma_{k}}-(\partial_{t}\xi_{d}^{m},u_{1}^{k}\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi_{d}^{m})_{1,\Sigma_{k}}

\displaystyle+(\partial_{t}\xi_{d}^{m},D_{t}(R^{n}v_{l}^{k})\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n})_{1,\Sigma_{k}}+(\partial_{1}(\mathcal{R}_{zz}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta^{k})\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi_{l}^{k}\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\eta^{k}),(D_{t}v_{d}^{m}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}))_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+[\mathcal{R}_{zz}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta^{k})\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi_{l}^{k}\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\eta^{k},D_{t}v_{d}^{m}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}]_{\ell},

\displaystyle II

\displaystyle=((v_{d}^{m},R^{n}D_{t}v_{d}^{m}))-((R^{n}v_{d}^{m},D_{t}v_{d}^{m}))-\int_{\Omega}\frac{\mu}{2}(\mathbb{D}_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}^{n}}v_{d}^{m}:\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{A}^{n}}(D_{t}v_{d}^{m})J^{n}

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega}\frac{\mu}{2}\Big(\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{A}^{n}}v_{d}^{m}:\mathbb{D}_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}^{n}}(D_{t}v_{d}^{m})+\partial_{t}J^{n}K^{n}\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{A}^{n}}v_{d}^{m}:\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{A}^{n}}(D_{t}v_{d}^{m})\Big)J^{n}

\displaystyle+((\partial_{t}(R^{n}v_{l}),D_{t}v_{d}^{m}))+((R^{n}v_{l},R^{n}D_{t}v_{d}^{m}))-\int_{\Omega}\frac{\mu}{2}\mathbb{D}_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}^{n}}(R^{n}v_{l}):\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{A}^{n}}(D_{t}v_{d}^{m})J^{n}

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega}\frac{\mu}{2}J^{n}\Big(\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{A}^{n}}(R^{n}v_{l}):\mathbb{D}_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}^{n}}(D_{t}v_{d}^{m})+\partial_{t}J^{n}K^{n}\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{A}^{n}}(R^{n}v_{l}):\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{A}^{n}}(D_{t}v_{d}^{m})\Big),

\displaystyle III

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\left[\partial_{t}F^{1}\cdot(D_{t}v_{d}^{m})+\partial_{t}J^{n}K^{n}F^{1}\cdot(D_{t}v_{d}^{m})+F^{1}\cdot R^{n}(D_{t}v_{d}^{m})\right]J^{n}-\int_{\Sigma}\partial_{t}F^{4}\cdot D_{t}v_{d}^{m}-F^{4}\cdot R^{n}D_{t}v_{d}^{m}

\displaystyle-\int_{\Sigma_{s}}\left[\partial_{t}F^{5}(D_{t}v_{d}^{m})+\partial_{t}J^{n}K^{n}F^{5}(D_{t}v_{d}^{m})+F^{5}R^{n}(D_{t}v_{d}^{m})\right]\cdot\tau J^{n}-[\partial_{t}F^{7},D_{t}v_{d}^{m}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}]_{\ell},

\displaystyle IV

\displaystyle=(\partial_{t}v_{d}^{m},R^{n}D_{t}v_{d}^{m})_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}-(\partial_{t}(R^{n}v_{d}^{m}),D_{t}v_{d}^{m})_{\mathcal{H}^{0}(\Omega)}+((R^{n}v_{d}^{m},D_{t}v_{d}^{m}))+(\partial_{t}^{2}(R^{n}v_{l}),D_{t}v_{d}^{m})_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}

\displaystyle+(\partial_{t}(R^{n}v_{l}),R^{n}D_{t}v_{d}^{m})_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}+(\partial_{t}(R^{n}v_{l}),D_{t}v_{d}^{m}\partial_{t}J^{n}(J^{n})^{-1})_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}.

We now estimate each term on the right-hand side of the second line in (
4.21
). We first estimate each term in
I
. From the fact that
|\mathcal{R}_{zz}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta)|\leq C
for some constant
C
, we obtain the following inequality

(4.22)

\displaystyle{}\int_{-\ell}^{\ell}\beta(\partial_{t}v_{d}^{m}\cdot\tau,(\partial_{t}v_{d}^{m}\cdot\tau)\partial_{t}J^{n})_{L^{2}(\Sigma_{s})}\lesssim||\partial_{t}J^{n}||_{L^{\infty}}||\partial_{t}v_{d}^{m}||^{2}_{L^{2}(\Sigma_{s})}\lesssim||\partial_{t}\eta^{n}||_{H^{\frac{3}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha}{2}}}||\partial_{t}v_{d}^{m}||_{H^{1}}^{2}.

For the terms in
(\cdot)_{1,\Sigma}
norm, we have:

(4.23)

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}\xi_{d}^{m},\partial_{t}u_{1}^{k}\partial_{t}\partial_{1}\xi_{l}^{k})_{1,\Sigma_{k}}\lesssim||\partial_{t}\xi_{d}^{m}||_{H^{1}}||\partial_{t}u_{1}^{k}||_{W^{1,+\infty}(\Sigma)}||\partial_{t}\xi_{l}^{k}||_{H^{2}}\lesssim||\partial_{t}\xi_{d}^{m}||_{H^{1}}||\partial_{t}u^{k}||_{H^{\frac{5}{2}}}||\partial_{t}\xi_{l}^{k}||_{H^{2}},

and

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}\xi_{d}^{m},u_{1}^{k}\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi_{d}^{m})_{1,\Sigma_{k}}

\displaystyle=\int_{-\ell}^{\ell}g\partial_{t}\xi_{d}^{m}u_{1}^{k}\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi_{d}^{m}+\sigma\frac{\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi_{d}^{m}u_{1}^{k}\partial_{1}^{2}\partial_{t}\xi_{d}^{m}}{(1+|\partial_{1}\zeta_{0}|^{2})^{3/2}}+\int_{-\ell}^{\ell}\mathcal{R}_{z}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta^{k})u_{1}^{k}\partial_{t}\partial_{1}\xi_{d}^{m}\partial_{1}^{2}\partial_{t}\xi_{d}^{m}

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{-\ell}^{\ell}g\partial_{t}\xi_{d}^{m}\partial_{1}u_{1}^{k}\partial_{t}\xi_{d}^{m}+\sigma\frac{\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi_{d}^{m}\partial_{1}u_{1}^{k}\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi_{d}^{m}}{(1+|\partial_{1}\zeta_{0}|^{2})^{3/2}}+\int_{-\ell}^{\ell}\mathcal{R}_{z}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta^{k})\partial_{1}u_{1}^{k}\partial_{t}\partial_{1}\xi_{d}^{m}\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi_{d}^{m}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\int_{-\ell}^{\ell}\mathcal{R}_{zz}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta^{k})u_{1}^{k}\partial_{t}\partial_{1}\xi_{d}^{m}\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi_{d}^{m}(\partial_{1}^{2}\zeta_{0}+\partial_{1}^{2}\eta^{k})

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\int_{-\ell}^{\ell}g\partial_{t}\xi_{d}^{m}\partial_{1}u_{1}^{k}\partial_{t}\xi_{d}^{m}+\sigma\frac{\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi_{d}^{m}\partial_{1}u_{1}^{k}\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi_{d}^{m}}{(1+|\partial_{1}\zeta_{0}|^{2})^{3/2}}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{-\ell}^{\ell}\mathcal{R}_{z}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta^{k})\partial_{1}u_{1}^{k}\partial_{t}\partial_{1}\xi_{d}^{m}\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi_{d}^{m}

(4.24)

\displaystyle\lesssim||\partial_{t}\xi_{d}^{m}||^{2}_{H^{1}}(||\eta^{k}||_{H^{3}}+||u^{k}||_{H^{\frac{5}{2}}}),

where we used integration by part, and the boundary terms vanish due to the fact
u_{1}(\pm\ell)=0
.

Finally, for the term including
v_{l}^{k},\xi_{l}^{k}
, by Hölder’s inequality, we have the following estimate

(4.25)

\displaystyle{}\begin{aligned} (\partial_{t}\xi_{d}^{m},D_{t}(R^{n}v_{l}^{k})\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n})_{1,\Sigma_{k}}&\lesssim\|\partial_{t}\xi_{d}^{m}\|_{H^{1}}\bigg(\|D_{t}v_{l}^{k}\|_{H^{1}(\Sigma)}\|\partial_{t}\eta^{n}\|_{W^{1,+\infty}}+\|v_{l}^{k}\|_{H^{1}(\Sigma)}\|\partial_{t}^{2}\eta^{n}\|_{W^{1,+\infty}}\bigg)\\
&\lesssim\|\partial_{t}\xi_{d}^{m}\|_{H^{1}}\bigg(\|D_{t}v_{l}^{k}\|_{H^{\frac{3}{2}}}\|\partial_{t}\eta^{n}\|_{W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{-}},q_{-}}}+\|v_{l}^{k}\|_{H^{\frac{3}{2}}}\|\partial_{t}^{2}\eta^{n}\|_{H^{\frac{5}{2}}}\bigg),\end{aligned}

(4.26)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &(\partial_{1}(\mathcal{R}_{zz}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta^{k})\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi_{l}^{k}\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\eta^{k}),(D_{t}v_{d}^{m}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}))_{L^{2}}+[\mathcal{R}_{zz}(\partial_{1}\zeta_{0},\partial_{1}\eta^{k})\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\xi_{l}^{k}\partial_{1}\partial_{t}\eta^{k},D_{t}v_{d}^{m}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}]_{\ell}\\
\lesssim&\|\partial_{t}\xi_{l}^{k}\|_{W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{-}},q_{-}}}\|\partial_{t}\eta^{k}\|_{H^{\frac{3}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha}{2}}}\|D_{t}v_{d}^{m}\|_{H^{1}}+\|\partial_{t}\eta^{k}\|_{W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{-}},q_{-}}}\|\partial_{t}\xi_{l}^{k}\|_{H^{\frac{3}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha}{2}}}\|D_{t}v_{d}^{m}\|_{H^{1}}\\
&+\|\partial_{t}\eta^{k}\|_{H^{\frac{3}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha}{2}}}\|\partial_{t}\xi_{l}^{k}\|_{H^{\frac{3}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha}{2}}}\|[D_{t}v_{d}^{m}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}]_{\ell}\|_{H^{1}}.\end{aligned}

In order to estimate terms in
II
, we use the relation

R^{n}\sim\nabla\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}^{n}+\nabla\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}^{n}\nabla\bar{\eta}^{n}\quad\text{and}\ \|J^{n}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 1
.
Applying Cauchy’s inequality, Sobolev’s inequality and the trace theory, we begin by estimating the term inside the
\mathcal{W}
inner product. We have

(4.27)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} ((v_{d}^{m},R^{n}D_{t}v_{d}^{m}))\lesssim&\int_{\Omega}|\nabla v_{d}^{m}||\nabla R^{n}||D_{t}v_{d}^{m}|+\int_{\Omega}|\nabla v_{d}^{m}||R^{n}||\nabla D_{t}v_{d}^{m}|\\
\lesssim&||v_{d}^{m}||_{H^{1}}||\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}^{n}||_{H^{2+\frac{\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha}{2}}}||D_{t}v_{d}^{m}||_{L^{\frac{4}{\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha}}}+||\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}^{n}||_{W^{1,+\infty}}||v_{d}^{m}||_{H^{1}}||D_{t}v_{d}^{m}||_{H^{1}}\\
\lesssim&||\partial_{t}{\eta}^{n}||_{H^{\frac{3}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha}{2}}}||v_{d}^{m}||_{H^{1}}||D_{t}v_{d}^{m}||_{H^{1}},\end{aligned}

and similarly

(4.28)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} ((R^{n}v_{d}^{m},D_{t}v_{d}^{m}))\lesssim||\partial_{t}{\eta}^{n}||_{H^{\frac{3}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha}{2}}}||v_{d}^{m}||_{H^{1}}||D_{t}v_{d}^{m}||_{H^{1}}.\end{aligned}

Then for other terms involved in II, we have the following estimate

(4.29)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\frac{\mu}{2}(\mathbb{D}_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}^{n}}v_{d}^{m}:\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{A}^{n}}(D_{t}v_{d}^{m}))J^{n}

\displaystyle\lesssim||D_{t}v_{d}^{m}||_{H^{1}}||v_{d}^{m}||_{H^{1}}||\partial_{t}\eta^{n}||_{H^{\frac{3}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha}{2}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim||D_{t}v_{d}^{m}||^{2}_{H^{1}}||\partial_{t}\eta^{n}||^{2}_{H^{\frac{3}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha}{2}}}+||v_{d}^{m}||_{H^{1}}^{2}.

Similarly, we have

(4.30)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\frac{\mu}{2}\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{A}^{n}}v_{d}^{m}:\mathbb{D}_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}^{n}}(D_{t}v_{d}^{m})\lesssim||D_{t}v_{d}^{m}||^{2}_{H^{1}}||\partial_{t}\eta^{n}||^{2}_{H^{\frac{3}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha}{2}}}+||v_{d}^{m}||_{H^{1}}^{2},

(4.31)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}J^{n}\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{A}^{n}}v_{d}^{m}:\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{A}}^{n}(D_{t}v_{d}^{m})\lesssim||D_{t}v_{d}^{m}||^{2}_{H^{1}}||\partial_{t}\eta||^{2}_{H^{\frac{3}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha}{2}}}+||v_{d}^{m}||_{H^{1}}^{2},

(4.32)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} ((\partial_{t}(R^{n}v_{l}),D_{t}v_{d}^{m}))\lesssim\|D_{t}v_{d}^{m}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}(\|\partial_{t}^{2}\eta^{n}\|_{W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{-}},q_{-}}}\|v_{l}^{k}\|_{H^{1}}+\|\partial_{t}\eta^{n}\|_{W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{-}},q_{-}}}\|D_{t}v_{l}^{k}\|_{H^{1}}),\end{aligned}

(4.33)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &((R^{n}D_{t}v_{l},R^{n}D_{t}v_{d}^{m}))\lesssim\|D_{t}v_{d}^{m}\|_{H^{1}}(\|\partial_{t}\eta^{n}\|_{W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{-}},q_{-}}}\|D_{t}v_{l}^{k}\|_{H^{1}})\\
&-\int_{\Omega}\frac{\mu}{2}(J^{n}\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{A}^{n}}(R^{n}v_{l}):\mathbb{D}_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}^{n}}(D_{t}v_{d}^{m})+\partial_{t}J^{n}K^{n}\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{A}^{n}}(R^{n}v_{l}):\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{A}^{n}}(D_{t}v_{d}^{m}))\\
&-\int_{\Omega}\frac{\mu}{2}(\mathbb{D}_{\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}^{n}}(R^{n}v_{l}):\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{A}^{n}}(D_{t}v_{d}^{m})J^{n}\lesssim||D_{t}v_{d}^{m}||^{2}_{H^{1}}||\partial_{t}\eta||^{2}_{H^{\frac{3}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha}{2}}}+\|\partial_{t}\eta^{n}\|_{W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{-}},q_{-}}}^{2}||v_{l}||_{H^{1}}^{2}.\end{aligned}

To estimate terms involved in
III
, we require more refined bounds, which we establish individually. We use the dual space estimate, the standard Sobolev embedding theorem and Hölder’s inequality to bound terms involving one time derivative of forces as follows

(4.34)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}F^{1}\cdot(D_{t}v_{d}^{m})J^{n}-\int_{-\ell}^{\ell}\partial_{t}F^{4}\cdot D_{t}v_{d}^{m}-\int_{\Sigma_{s}}\partial_{t}F^{5}(D_{t}v_{d}^{m})\cdot\tau J^{n}\lesssim\|\partial_{t}(F^{1}-F^{4}-F^{5})\|_{(\mathcal{H}^{1})^{\ast}}\|D_{t}v_{d}^{m}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}.\end{aligned}

For terms involving
F^{1}
, we estimate them by Hölder’s inequality with
\frac{1}{q_{-}}+\frac{\varepsilon_{-}}{2}=1
, and Sobolev embedding
H^{1}\hookrightarrow L^{\varepsilon_{-}/2}

(4.35)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \int_{\Omega}\left[\partial_{t}J^{n}K^{n}F^{1}\cdot(D_{t}v_{d}^{m})+F^{1}\cdot R^{n}(D_{t}v_{d}^{m})\right]J^{n}&\lesssim(\|\partial_{t}J^{n}K^{n}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|R^{n}\|_{L^{\infty}})\|F^{1}\|_{L^{q_{-}}(\Omega)}\|D_{t}v_{d}^{m}\|_{L^{\varepsilon_{-}/2}(\Omega)}\\
&\lesssim\|\partial_{t}\eta^{n}\|_{H^{3/2+(\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha)/2}}\|F^{1}\|_{L^{q_{-}}(\Omega)}(\|D_{t}v_{d}^{m}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}).\end{aligned}

Next, we estimate the integral involving
F^{4}
. By Hölder’s inequality and the Sobolev embedding

W^{1-1/q_{-},q_{-}}(\Sigma)\hookrightarrow L^{1/\varepsilon_{-}}(\Sigma)\quad\text{and}\ H^{1/2}(\Sigma)\hookrightarrow L^{1/\varepsilon_{-}}(\Sigma)
,
and trace theory, we have

(4.36)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \int_{-\ell}^{\ell}F^{4}\cdot R^{n}(D_{t}v_{d}^{m})&\lesssim\|F^{4}\|_{L^{1/(1-\varepsilon_{-})}(\Sigma)}\|R^{n}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma)}(\|D_{t}v_{d}^{m}\|_{L^{1/\varepsilon_{-}}(\Sigma)})\\
&\lesssim\|\partial_{t}\eta^{n}\|_{H^{3/2+(\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha)/2}}\|F^{4}\|_{W^{1-1/q_{-},q_{-}}}(\|D_{t}v_{d}^{m}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}).\end{aligned}

The integral involving
F^{5}
is bounded similarly as
F^{4}
via

(4.37)

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{s}}\left[\partial_{t}J^{n}K^{n}F^{5}(D_{t}v_{d}^{m})+F^{5}R^{n}(D_{t}v_{d}^{m})\right]\cdot\tau J^{n}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\|\partial_{t}J^{n}K^{n}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{s})}+\|R^{n}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Sigma_{s})})\|F^{5}\|_{L^{1/(1-\varepsilon_{-})}(\Sigma_{s})}(\|D_{t}v_{d}^{m}\|_{L^{1/\varepsilon_{-}}(\Sigma_{s})})

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\partial_{t}\eta^{n}\|_{H^{3/2+(\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha)/2}}\|F^{5}\|_{W^{1-1/q_{-},q_{-}}}(\|D_{t}v_{d}^{m}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}).

Finally, the estimate for the term involving
F^{7}
is straightforward to estimate as follows

[\partial_{t}F^{7},D_{t}v_{d}^{m}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}]_{\ell}\lesssim[\partial_{t}F^{7}]_{\ell}[D_{t}v_{d}^{m}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}]_{\ell}.

For the terms involved in
IV
, they can be estimated as follows

(4.38)

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}v_{d}^{m},R^{n}D_{t}v_{d}^{m})_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\lesssim||\partial_{t}{\eta}^{n}||_{H^{\frac{3}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha}{2}}}||D_{t}v_{d}^{m}||^{2}_{L^{2}}+||\partial_{t}\eta^{n}||_{H^{\frac{3}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha}{2}}}^{2}||v_{d}^{m}||_{L^{2}}||D_{t}v_{d}^{m}||_{L^{2}},

and

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}(R^{n}v_{d}^{m}),D_{t}v_{d}^{m})_{L^{2}(\Omega)}=

\displaystyle||D_{t}v_{d}^{m}||_{L^{2}}||\partial_{t}v_{d}^{m}||_{L^{2}}||\partial_{t}\eta^{n}||_{H^{\frac{3}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha}{2}}}+||\partial_{t}^{2}\eta^{n}||_{H^{\frac{3}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha}{2}}}||v_{d}^{m}||_{L^{2}(\Omega)}||D_{t}v_{d}^{m}||_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle||D_{t}v_{d}^{m}||^{2}_{L^{2}}||\partial_{t}\eta^{n}||_{H^{\frac{3}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha}{2}}}+||\partial_{t}^{2}\eta^{n}||_{H^{\frac{3}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha}{2}}}||v_{d}^{m}||_{L^{2}(\Omega)}||D_{t}v_{d}^{m}||_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

(4.39)

\displaystyle+||D_{t}v_{d}^{m}||_{L^{2}}||v_{d}^{m}||_{L^{2}}||\partial_{t}\eta^{n}||_{H^{\frac{3}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon_{-}-\alpha}{2}}}^{2},

(4.40)

\displaystyle((R^{n}v_{d}^{m},D_{t}v_{d}^{m}))\lesssim\|D_{t}v_{d}^{m}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}\|R^{n}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|v_{d}^{m}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}\lesssim\|D_{t}v_{d}^{m}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}\eta^{n}\|_{W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{-}},q_{-}}}\|v_{d}^{m}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)},

(4.41)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &(\partial_{t}^{2}(R^{n}v_{l}),D_{t}v_{d}^{m})_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}\\
\lesssim&\|D_{t}v_{d}^{m}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\bigg(\|\partial_{t}^{3}\bar{\eta}^{n}\|_{H^{1}}\|v_{l}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}+\|\partial_{t}^{2}\bar{\eta}^{n}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}v_{l}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}^{n}\|_{H^{2-\alpha}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}^{2}v_{l}\|_{H^{1}}\bigg)\\
\lesssim&\|D_{t}v_{d}^{m}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\bigg(\|\partial_{t}^{3}\bar{\eta}^{n}\|_{H^{1}}\|v_{l}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}}+\|\partial_{t}^{2}\bar{\eta}^{n}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}v_{l}\|_{W^{2,q_{-}}}+\|\partial_{t}\bar{\eta}^{n}\|_{H^{2-\alpha}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}^{2}v_{l}\|_{H^{1}}\bigg),\end{aligned}

and similarly,

(4.42)

\displaystyle{}\begin{aligned} (\partial_{t}(R^{n}v_{l}),R^{n}v_{d}^{m})_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}\lesssim&\|\partial_{t}\eta^{n}\|_{W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{-}},q_{-}}}\|v_{d}^{m}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}\eta^{n}\|_{W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{-}},q_{-}}}\|\partial_{t}v_{l}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\\
&+\|\partial_{t}\eta^{n}\|_{W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{-}},q_{-}}}\|v_{d}^{m}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}^{2}\eta^{n}\|_{H^{1}}\|v_{l}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}(\Omega)},\\
(\partial_{t}(R^{n}v_{l}),\partial_{t}J^{n}(J^{n})^{-1}v_{d}^{m})_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}\lesssim&\|\partial_{t}\eta^{n}\|_{W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{-}},q_{-}}}\|v_{d}^{m}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}\eta^{n}\|_{W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{-}},q_{-}}}\|\partial_{t}v_{l}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\\
&+\|\partial_{t}\eta^{n}\|_{W^{3-\frac{1}{q_{-}},q_{-}}}\|v_{d}^{m}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}^{2}\eta^{n}\|_{H^{1}}\|v_{l}\|_{W^{2,q_{+}}(\Omega)}.\end{aligned}

Thus, by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and by combining (
4.22
)–(
4.42
), we conclude the energy structure

(4.43)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\frac{d}{dt}\frac{1}{2}\left(\|D_{t}v_{d}^{m}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{0}}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}\xi_{d}^{m}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\right)+\frac{1}{4}\|D_{t}v_{d}^{m}\|_{\mathcal{H}^{1}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}[D_{t}v_{d}^{m}\cdot\mathcal{N}^{n}]_{\ell}^{2}\\
